What did it all mean? Could the *Gazette* explain all this?

===Chinese===
我们把那块宣告我们的棱堡“不可征服”的牌子连钉子一齐拔了下来，好把这件物证随身带走——它见证的是我们那份情有可原的狂傲。日暮时分，工事弃守，只留下四十人守到最后一刻、能放多少枪就放多少枪——用我们队长的话说，凡是能叫敌人折损一两个人的机会，一个也不许放过。晚上八点钟左右，我们从皮诺塔上望见那四十人撤退下来：他们先用刺刀迎了迎入侵者，然后一路苦战，英勇地退了下来。

棱堡内部的地雷没有起多大作用，大桥上的小地雷倒是不辱使命：通道被炸毁，棱堡与韦尔瓦河对岸隔绝开来。法军一旦把这个阵地连同圣何塞一并到手，便有了足够的掩护来开辟第三道平行壕，从容不迫地把全城一圈城墙逐一轰平。我们心里悲伤，也有点儿泄气；可是第二天就有热闹可看、有庆典可过，一点点意气消沉又算得了什么？疯也似的沮丧过一场之后，来点儿欢乐倒也不坏，尤其是在连掩埋死人的工夫都腾不出来、房子里也容不下那许多伤员的时候。千真万确，凡是该做的事都有的是人手——感谢上帝。

举城欢腾的缘由是：种种光荣的传闻正不胫而走，说西班牙的各路大军踩着法军的脚后跟，正从半岛各地赶来救援我们。百姓们拥进塞奥广场，聚在拉马格达莱纳拱门（Magdalene arch）前，一心等候《公报》出版；《公报》终于出来了，人人精神为之一振，颗颗心都让希望激得怦怦直跳。这些传闻究竟是当真传到了萨拉戈萨，还是出自主编唐·伊格纳西奥·阿索尔（Don Ignacio Assor）那颗灵光的脑袋，我不得而知。可以肯定的是，白纸黑字告诉了我们：雷丁（Reading）正率六万大军前来解围；拉桑侯爵（Marquis of Lazán）在加泰罗尼亚北部击溃匪兵之后，已经攻入法国，正向四面八方播散恐怖；因凡塔多公爵（Duke del Infantado）也在赶来相助——他与布莱克（Blake）、拉罗曼纳（la Romana）合力，已经把拿破仑打得大败，斩敌两万，贝尔蒂埃（Berthier）、内伊（Ney）、萨瓦里（Savary）一个不剩尽数毙命；又有英国人运抵加的斯（Cádiz）的数百万现银，专充战费。这一切到底算怎么回事？《公报》能把这一切统统自圆其说吗？

===Original===
In spite of the size of these mouthfuls of rumor, we swallowed them; and there were demonstrations of joy, ringing of church bells, running through the streets, and singing the music of the jota, with many other patriotic excesses, which at least had the advantage of affording us a little of that cooling off of our mental temperature which was necessary. Do not believe that in consideration of our joy the rain of bombs had ceased! Very far from that! They seemed to jeer at the news of our *Gazette*, as they repeated their dose. Feeling a lively desire to laugh at them to their faces, we went to the walls. The musicians of the regiments played in a tantalizing fashion, and we all sang in an immense chorus the famous words﻿—

> The Virgin del Pilar says
 

 She wouldn’t like to be a Frenchwoman!

They were in a mood for answering jests, and in less than two hours a greater number of projectiles were sent into the city than during all the rest of the day. There was now no longer a secure refuge; there was not a hand’s breadth of ground or of roof free from that Satanic fire. Families fled from their homes, or took refuge in the cellars. The wounded, who were numerous in the principal houses, were carried to the churches, seeking shelter in their strong vaults. Others went dragging themselves along. Some more active ones carried their bedding upon their shoulders. Most of them were accommodated in the Pilar; and after the floor was all filled, they were stretched out upon the altars and crowded into the chapels. In spite of their misfortunes, they were consoled by looking at the Virgin, who seemed to say to them unceasingly with her brilliant eyes that she would not care to be a Frenchwoman!

===Chinese===
这些传闻的块头再大，我们也一口一口囫囵吞了下去；于是有了种种欢庆的表示：教堂钟声齐鸣，满街奔走欢呼，奏起霍塔舞的调子，还有旁的许多爱国的过头举动——这些举动至少有一样好处：给我们那亟需冷却的头脑温度稍稍降了降温。千万别以为法军看在我们欢喜的份上，就让那场炸弹雨停了！差得远呢！他们仿佛在讥笑我们《公报》上的新闻，照旧把他们那一剂加量奉上。我们正怀着强烈的心愿要当着他们的面回敬一场嘲笑，便登上了城墙。各团的乐手们挑逗似的奏起乐来，我们大家便用一片浩大的合唱，齐声唱起那两句名言——

> 皮拉尔圣母说道

 她可不想当法国女人！

他们倒也有心应答这场玩笑：不到两个钟头，打进城里的炮弹，比那一天其余时候的总数还要多。城里从此再没有一处可靠的避难之所了；一掌宽的地面、一掌宽的屋顶，都躲不开那撒旦般的炮火。各家各户纷纷逃出家门，或者躲进地窖。各主要宅邸里的大批伤员被抬往各处教堂，到坚固的穹顶底下去寻求庇护。另一些人则自己拖着伤躯一路挪过去；手脚利索些的，把铺盖扛在肩头。他们大都被安顿在皮拉尔圣母教堂里；地面躺满之后，人们便躺上了祭坛，挤进了一间间小堂。尽管遭逢种种不幸，他们望着圣母便得了安慰——圣母仿佛正用她那双明亮的眼睛，不住地对他们说：她可不愿意当法国女人！

## XII / 第十二章

===Original===
My battalion did not take part in the sorties of the days of the twenty-second and twenty-fourth, nor in the defence of the Molino and the positions situated at the back of San José, made glorious by the destruction of many of our troops, where they had made the French feel the strength of their hand. It was not because they had not been careful to take precautions, for indeed from the mouth of Huerva to the Carmen gate they stationed fifty cannon, most of them of heavy calibre, directing them with great skill against our weakest points. In spite of all this, we laughed, or pretended to laugh, at them, as in the vainglorious response of Palafox to Marshal Lannes (who had placed himself since the twenty-second at the head of the besieging army), in which he said to him, “The conquest of this city will be a great honor to Monsieur the Marshal if he gains it in open fight, and not with bombs and grenades, which only terrify cowards.”

Of course, after a few days had passed, it was known that the hoped-for forces and the powerful armies that were coming to free us were all mists of our imaginations, and especially of that of the journalist who invented them. There were no such armies of any sort roaming about to help us.

I understood very soon that all that which was published in the *Gazette* of the sixteenth was a canard, and so I said to Don José de Montoria and his wife, who in their optimism attributed my incredulity to a lack of public spirit. I had gone with Augustine and others of my friends to the Montoria house to help them at a task that was wearying them greatly. A part of their roof had been destroyed by the bombs, and this threatened the walls with destruction also. They were trying to remedy this with all possible speed. The eldest son of Montoria, wounded in battle at the Molino, had been lodged with his wife and son in the cellar of a house close by, and Doña Leocadia gave her hands and feet no rest, going and coming between the two houses, carrying things which were necessary.

===Chinese===
二十二日、二十四日两天的出击战，我所在的营没有份；守卫莫利诺（Molino）磨坊和圣何塞背后诸阵地的战斗，我们也没有参加——在那里，我军许多部队以惨重的伤亡换得了荣名，也教法国人尝到了他们铁腕的厉害。我们之所以得手，倒不是法军疏于防范；的确，从韦尔瓦河口到卡门城门，他们摆下了五十门大炮，多半是大口径的重炮，又以极精巧的手段，把它们对准我们最薄弱之处。尽管如此，我们照样嘲笑他们，或者装出嘲笑的样子——就像帕拉福克斯答复拉纳元帅的那句逞豪之语（元帅自二十二日起已亲任围城大军统帅）。他对元帅说：“若凭堂堂之阵的交锋攻下此城，对元帅先生才是一份极大的荣耀；靠炸弹和手榴弹可不成——那玩意儿只能吓唬懦夫。”

当然，没过几天，大家便都知道了：那些我们望眼欲穿的援军、那些前来解围的威武之师，统统是我们的想象编织出来的迷雾，尤其是那位凭空编造出它们来的报人的想象。这样的军队一支也不存在，根本没有谁在四处游荡前来救我们。

十六日那一期《公报》（*Gazette*）上刊载的消息全是凭空捏造的谣言，我很快就看破了，并且照实对唐·何塞·德·蒙特里亚夫妇说了；他们一味乐观，反倒把我的不敢轻信归咎于我缺少公民热忱。我是同奥古斯丁和另外几个朋友一起到蒙特里亚宅上去的，去帮他们做一件让他们心力交瘁的活计。宅子的屋顶被炮弹掀掉了一部分，眼看墙体也有被牵连坍塌之虞，他们正竭尽全力火速抢修。蒙特里亚的长子在莫利诺磨坊之战中挂了彩，带着妻小寄住到近旁一户人家的地窖里；堂娜·莱奥卡迪娅手脚一刻不得闲，两座宅子之间来来回回，搬运着种种必需的物件。

===Original===
“I can’t let anything be done by others,” she said to me; “that is my nature. Although I have servants, I am not content unless I do everything myself. How has my son Augustine borne himself?”

“Like what he is, señora, a brave boy,” I answered; “and his talent for war is so great that I should not be surprised to see him a general in a couple of years.”

“A general!” she exclaimed in surprise. “My son is going to chant masses as soon as the siege is ended. Indeed you know we have educated him for that. God and the Virgin del Pilar bring him in safety through battle, that the rest of his days may go on in appointed ways! The fathers at the Seminary have assured me that I shall see my son with his mitre on his head and his crosier in his hand.”

“It will be so, señora, I do not doubt it. But seeing how he manages arms, I cannot bring myself to the thought that with the same hand with which he pulls the trigger, he will also scatter benedictions.”

“It is true, Señor de Araceli; and I have always said that the trigger is not becoming to churchmen. But you see how it is. Here we have great warriors﻿—Don Santiago Sas, Don Manuel Lasartesa; the incumbent of San Pablo, Don Antonio La Casa; the parish priest of San Miguel, Don José Martínez; and also Don Vicente Casanova, who is famous as the first theologian of Saragossa. Indeed they all fight, my son also, though I suppose he will be eager to return to the Seminary and plunge into his studies. Would you believe it? Lately he was studying books so large that they weighed two quintals. God’s blessing be on the boy! I am quite foolish over him when he recites some grand things all in Latin. I suppose they are all about our Lord, and his love for his church, because there is a great deal about *amorem* and *formosa* and *pulcherrima*, *inflamavit*, and other words like those.”

===Chinese===
“什么事都得亲手过一遍我才放心，”她对我说，“我这人生来如此。家里虽使唤着仆人，可凡事不经我亲手操办，心里就不踏实。我儿子奥古斯丁表现如何？”

“夫人，他表现得就像他的本相——一条好汉，”我答道，“而且他的军事才能如此了得，过上一两年，若是我见他当上了将军，我也不会惊讶。”

“将军！”她惊呼起来。“我儿子围城一结束就要去诵唱弥撒了。您是知道的，我们栽培他正是为了这个。愿上帝和皮拉尔圣母保佑他平安穿过战阵，好叫他后半辈子循着注定的轨道走下去！神学院的神父们向我担保过，我准能看到我儿子头戴主教冠、手执权杖。”

“一定会的，夫人，我毫不怀疑。只是眼看他把兵器摆弄得那样在行，我实在难以想象：同一只扣扳机的手，日后还要用来播撒祝福。”

“说得是啊，阿拉塞利先生；我也常说，扳机这东西与神职人员的身份不相称。可您瞧瞧如今的情形。我们这里能征惯战的教士多着呢——圣地亚哥·萨斯、唐·曼努埃尔·拉萨尔特萨（Don Manuel Lasartesa）、圣巴勃罗堂的本堂神父唐·安东尼奥·拉卡萨（Don Antonio La Casa）、圣米格尔堂的本堂神父唐·何塞·马丁内斯（Don José Martínez），还有唐·维森特·卡萨诺瓦（Don Vicente Casanova），那位萨拉戈萨首屈一指的神学家。说真的，他们个个都在打仗，我儿子也不例外；不过我料想他正一心惦着回神学院埋头做学问呢。您信不信？他近来钻研的那些书，一本本大得足有两公担（quintal）重。愿上帝赐福这孩子！每当他用拉丁文高声背诵些冠冕堂皇的大文章，我就欢喜得没了魂儿。我猜那都是讲我们主、讲他如何挚爱教会的，因为里头满纸都是 *amorem*（爱）呀、*formosa*（美人）呀、*pulcherrima*（至美）呀、*inflamavit*（点燃）呀这一类字眼。”

===Original===
“Exactly,” I replied, imagining that his recitations were from the fourth book of a certain ecclesiastical work called the *Aeneid*, written by a certain Friar Virgil of the order of Predicadores.

“It must be as I say,” said Doña Leocadia. “And now, Señor de Araceli, let us see if you can help me move this table.”

“With the greatest pleasure, dear lady. I will move it for you myself,” I replied, taking charge of it at the moment that Don José de Montoria entered, pouring out “*porras*” and “*cuernos*” from his blessed mouth.

“How is this, *porra*!” he cried; “men occupied in women’s business? It is not for moving tables and chairs that a gun has been placed in your hands! And you, wife? How can you distract in this manner a man needed on the other side? You and the children, *porra*! can you not move the furniture? Are you made of paste or cheese? Look! In the street below is the Countess de Bureta with a bed on her shoulders, and her two maids carrying a wounded soldier on a cot.”

“Very well,” said Doña Leocadia, “there is no need of making such a noise about it. The men may go. Everybody out into the street, and leave us alone! Away with you, too, Augustine my son, and God preserve you in the midst of this inferno.”

===Chinese===
“可不是嘛，”我答道，心里却在想，他背诵的那些宏文，出自某部教会典籍的第四卷，书名叫《埃涅阿斯纪》（*Aeneid*），作者是传道修会（Predicadores）的一位维吉尔修士。

“准是我说的这样，”堂娜·莱奥卡迪娅道，“好了，阿拉塞利先生，且看看您能不能帮我搬一搬这张桌子。”

“万分乐意，亲爱的夫人。我这就亲自替您搬，”我答着，正要伸手接这差事，唐·何塞·德·蒙特里亚走了进来，从他那张宝贵的嘴里接连喷出“*porras*”（波拉）和“*cuernos*”（库埃尔诺，意即“见鬼”）来。

“这是搞的什么名堂，*波拉*！”他嚷道，“大男人们干娘儿们的活计？发一支枪到你们手里，可不是叫你们来搬桌挪椅的！还有你，老婆子？城那头正用得着人手，你怎么能这样耽误人家一条汉子？你和孩子们，*波拉*！家具你们自己搬不动？你们是面团捏的，还是奶酪做的？瞧瞧！底下街上，布雷塔伯爵夫人正肩上扛着一张床，她的两个使女用一张抬床抬着个伤兵。”

“很好，”堂娜·莱奥卡迪娅说，“犯不着为这个这么大呼小叫。男人们可以走了。都给我上街去，让我们清静清静！你也走吧，我的儿子奥古斯丁，愿上帝保佑你平安穿过这座炼狱。”

===Original===
“We must carry twenty sacks of flour from the Convent of Trinitarios to the headquarters of supplies,” said Montoria. “Come, let us all go.”

And when we were in the street, he added, “The numbers of people in Saragossa will soon make half rations necessary. It is true, my friends, that there is much concealed provision, and although it has been ordered that everybody declare what he has, many do not take any notice of the order, and keep what they have to sell at fabulous prices. It’s a bad business. If I discover them, and they fall into my hands, I will make them understand that Montoria is president of the junta of supplies.”

We had reached the parish church of San Pablo when we were met by a friar, Father Mateo del Busto, who was coming with much fatigue, forcing his feeble steps, and accompanied by another friar whom they called Father Luengo.

===Chinese===
“我们得把二十袋面粉从三一会修道院运到补给总部去，”蒙特里亚说，“来，大伙儿一起去。”

到了街上，他又说：“萨拉戈萨人口这么多，很快就要实行口粮减半了。朋友们，实情是，城里私藏的存粮还多得很；虽说已经下令人人申报家中的存粮，许多人却压根不理会这道命令，把囤着的粮食攥在手里，等着卖天价。这可是桩糟糕透顶的事。要是叫我查出来、落在我手里，我会让他们明白明白：蒙特里亚是补给议会主席。”

我们走到圣巴勃罗堂教堂跟前，迎面遇上一位修士——马特奥·德尔·布斯托神父。他满脸疲态，拖着虚软的步子勉强走来，身旁还跟着另一位修士，人称卢恩戈神父（Father Luengo）。

===Original===
“What news do your reverences bring us?” Montoria asked them.

“Don Juan Gallart has twenty pounds of inlaid work which he places at the disposal of the committee.”

“And Don Pedro Pizueta, the shopkeeper of the Calle de las Moscas, generously offers sixty sacks of wool, and all the salt and wool of his storehouses,” added Luengo.

“But we have just been dealing with the miser Candiola,” said the friar; “a battle with which not even the Eras can compare.”

“How is that?” asked Don José, with astonishment. “Has not that wretched niggard understood that we will pay him for his flour? He is the only citizen of Saragossa who has not given a penny for the provisioning of the army.”

===Chinese===
“两位神父可给我们带来了什么消息？”蒙特里亚问他们。

“唐·胡安·加利亚特（Don Juan Gallart）有二十磅镶嵌工艺品，愿交由议会支配。”

“还有拉斯莫斯卡斯街（Calle de las Moscas）的店主唐·佩德罗·皮苏埃塔（Don Pedro Pizueta），慷慨献出六十包羊毛，外加他货栈里全部的盐和羊毛。”卢恩戈补充道。

“可是我们刚刚跟吝啬鬼坎迪奥拉交过一 回手，”那位修士说，“那一场恶战，连埃拉斯之战都比不上。”

“这是怎么说？”唐·何塞惊愕地问，“那个可鄙的守财奴还不明白，我们会照价付他面粉钱吗？全萨拉戈萨的市民，唯独他一个，为军队筹粮没出过一分一厘。”

===Original===
“There is no use in preaching to Candiola,” said Luengo. “He has said decisively that we need not return there unless we bring him one hundred and twenty-four reales for each sack of flour, and he has seventy-eight of them in his storehouse.”

“Is there any infamy equal to his!” exclaimed Montoria, letting loose a string of *porras*, which I do not copy for fear of wearying my reader. “What! A hundred and twenty-four reales are necessary to make that stingy piece of flint understand the duties of a son of Saragossa in times like these! The Captain-General has given me authority to take whatever provisions are necessary, paying the fixed price for them.”

“Do you hear what I tell you, Señor Don José?” said Busto; “Candiola says that who wants flour must pay for it. He said that if the city is not able to defend itself, it must surrender; that he has no obligation to give anything for the war, because he was not the one who brought it on.”

“Let us go there,” said Montoria, with anger, which showed itself in his gestures, his altered voice, his darkened visage. “It is not the first time that I have had that dog, that bloodsucker, in my hands.”

===Chinese===
“跟坎迪奥拉讲道是白费唇舌，”卢恩戈说，“他斩钉截铁地放了话：每袋面粉不带来一百二十四里亚尔，我们就休再登他家的门；他库房里还囤着七十八袋呢。”

“天下还有比这更无耻的吗！”蒙特里亚嚷道，顺口放出一连串 *波拉*——我怕读者听着厌烦，就不一一照录了。“什么！竟非得拿一百二十四里亚尔，才能叫那块吝啬的燧石疙瘩明白，如今的时世里一个萨拉戈萨之子应尽什么本分！总司令已经授了权给我：凡属必需的粮秣，尽可征用，按官价付钱。”

“您听见我的话了吧，唐·何塞先生？”布斯托说，“坎迪奥拉放话说：谁要面粉，谁掏钱。他还说，这城要是自己守不住，就该投降；这场战争又不是他招来的，他没有义务为它掏一个子儿。”

“走，找他去，”蒙特里亚说，怒气从他的手势、他变了调的嗓音、他阴沉下来的脸上直往外冒。“那条狗、那个吸血鬼落到我手里，这可不是头一回了。”

===Original===
I came behind with Augustine, who was pale and downcast. I wished to speak with him, but he made signs to keep silence. We followed to see how this would end. We found ourselves quickly in the Calle de Antón Trillo; and Montoria said to us﻿—

“Boys, go on ahead and knock at the door of this insolent Jew. Force it open, if no one opens it; enter, and tell him to come down to see me. Take him by the ear, but be careful he does not bite you, for he is a mad dog and a venomous serpent.”

When we were walking on, I looked again at Augustine, and saw that he was livid and trembling.

“Gabriel,” he said, “I wish to run away. I wish that the earth would open and swallow me. My father will kill me, but I cannot do what he has commanded me.”

“Come, lean on me; then act as if you had twisted your foot, and cannot go on.”

===Chinese===
我同奥古斯丁落在后面，他脸色发白，垂头丧气。我想跟他说句话，他却直摆手，示意我别作声。我们跟着大伙儿，且看这事如何收场。不一会儿便到了安东·特里约街，蒙特里亚对我们说——

“小伙子们，你们打前站，去敲这个无礼的犹太佬的门。没人开门就把门砸开；进去，叫他下来见我。揪住他的耳朵，可当心别叫他咬着，他是一条疯狗，又是一条毒蛇。”

再往前走的时候，我又看了奥古斯丁一眼，只见他面如死灰，浑身发抖。

“加夫列尔，”他说，“我想逃走。我恨不得大地裂开口子把我吞下去。父亲会杀了我的，可他吩咐我的事，我办不到。”

“来，靠着我；待会儿就装作崴了脚，走不动路了。”

===Original===
This was done, and our other companions and I began knocking at the door. The old woman showed herself at the window, and greeted us with a thousand insolent words. A few minutes passed, and then we saw a very beautiful hand raise the curtain, permitting us to see for a moment a face changed and pale, whose great dark eyes cast terrified glances towards the street.

At that moment my companions and the boys who were following were crying in hoarse concert﻿—

“Come down, uncle Candiola. Come down, dog of a Caiaphas!”

Contrary to our expectation, Candiola obeyed; but he did it believing that he had to do with the mob of vagabond boys who were in the habit of giving him such serenades, with no suspicion that the president of the junta of supplies, and two others in authority, were there to talk with him on a matter of importance. He soon had occasion to know that this was a serious matter, for at the opening of the door, as he came running out with a cudgel in his hand, and his ugly eyes glowing with wrath, he came face to face with Montoria, and drew back in alarm.

===Chinese===
依计而行。我和其余的同伴们便上前去敲门。那老妇人在窗口露了面，用一千句无礼的话“迎接”我们。过了几分钟，只见一只极美的手掀起窗帘，让我们霎时间瞥见一张苍白而变了模样的脸，一双乌黑的大眼睛正向街上投来惊惶的一瞥。

就在这当口，我的同伴们和跟来的顽童们正扯着嘶哑的嗓子齐声呐喊——

“下来，坎迪奥拉大叔！下来，该亚法（Caiaphas）老狗！”

出乎我们的意料，坎迪奥拉居然服从了；不过他下来时只道又是那帮惯常给他来这类“小夜曲”的流浪顽童，万万没想到补给议会主席和另外两位有身份的人物正候在门外，要同他商谈要事。他很快就领教了这不是儿戏：门一开，他手攥木棍、一双丑眼里燃着怒火冲将出来，迎面正撞上蒙特里亚，吓得倒退了一步。

===Original===
“Ah, it is you, Señor de Montoria,” he said, with very bad grace. “How is it that you, being a member of the committee of public safety, have not been able to disperse this rabble which has come to make this noise before the gate of the house of an honorable citizen?”

“I am not a member of the committee of public safety, but of the junta of supplies, so I come in search of the Señor Candiola, and make him come down; but I will not enter this dark house full of cobwebs and mice.”

“The poor cannot live in palaces like Señor José de Montoria, administrator of the goods of the commune, and for a long time tax-collector,” replied Candiola.

“I made my fortune by work, not by usury,” exclaimed Montoria. “But let us make an end of this. Señor Don Jerónimo, I have come for that flour. These two good fathers have acquainted you with our need of it already.”

===Chinese===
“啊，是您哪，德·蒙特里亚先生，”他没好气地说，“您这位治安委员会的委员，怎么连这起子泼皮都驱散不了？他们跑到一个体面市民的家门口来这样大呼小叫。”

“我不是治安委员会的委员，我是补给议会的；我是来找坎迪奥拉先生、叫他下来的。不过您这座黑黢黢、结满蛛网、老鼠成群的宅子，我是决计不进去的。”

“穷人可住不进德·蒙特里亚先生那样的宫殿——您可是公共资财的经管大员，还当了那么多年的税官。”坎迪奥拉回敬道。

“我的家业是干活挣来的，不是放印子钱盘剥来的！”蒙特里亚喝道，“闲话少说。唐·赫罗尼莫先生，我是为那批面粉来的。这两位好神父想必已经把我们的急需向您交代过了。”

===Original===
“Yes, I will sell it, I will sell it,” answered Candiola, with a crafty smile; “but I cannot part with it at the price which these señors indicated. It is too little. I do not part with it for less than one hundred and sixty-two reales for a sack of a hundred pounds.”

“I do not ask *your* price,” said Don José, restraining his indignation.

“The junta may dispose as it likes with its own; but in my house no one sells anything but myself,” answered the miser. “And that is all there is to say. Each one may do in his own house as I do in mine.”

“Come, look here, you bloodsucker!” exclaimed Montoria, catching him by the arm, making him jump, “look here, Candiola of a thousand devils, I have said that I have come for the flour, and I will not go without it! The army of defence of Saragossa must not die of hunger, *porra*! and all citizens must contribute to maintain it.”

===Chinese===
“卖，我卖，我卖，”坎迪奥拉答道，脸上堆起狡黠的笑，“可照这几位先生开的价，我不能出手。太少了。一袋一百磅，少于一百六十二里亚尔我不撒手。”

“我没有问*您*的价，”唐·何塞说，强压着满腔怒火。

“议会爱拿自己的东西怎么处置随它的便；可在我的家里，除了我本人，谁也卖不了任何东西，”守财奴答道，“话就说到这儿。各人在自己家里想怎么着就怎么着，一如我在我家里。”

“来，你给我听着，你这吸血鬼！”蒙特里亚嚷道，一把攥住他的胳膊，搡得他一个趔趄，“你给我听着，千鬼缠身的坎迪奥拉，我说过了，我是为面粉来的，不拿到面粉就不走！萨拉戈萨的守军绝不能饿死，*波拉*！全体市民都得出力供养它。”

===Original===
“To maintain it! to maintain the army!” cried the miser, venomously. “Perhaps I am the author of its being?”

“Miserable pig, is there not in your black and empty soul one spark of patriotism?”

“I do not maintain vagabonds. What need was there that the French should bombard us and destroy the city? You want me to feed the soldiers. I will give them poison.”

“Wretch, worm, bloodsucker of Saragossa, disgrace of the Spanish people!” exclaimed my protector, threatening with his doubled fist the miser’s wrinkled face. “I would rather be damned to hell forever than to be what you are, to be Candiola for one minute. You black conscience, you perverse soul, are you not ashamed of being the only one in this city who has refused all his resources to the patriotic army of his country? Does not everybody’s hatred of you for this vile conduct weigh upon you more heavily than if all the rocks of Moncayo had fallen upon you?”

===Chinese===
“供养它！供养军队！”守财奴咬牙切齿地喊道，“难道这支军队是我生出来的不成？”

“卑贱的猪猡，你那颗又黑又空的灵魂里，当真没有一星半点的爱国火种？”

“我不养流氓。当初又何苦招惹法国人来炮轰我们、毁掉这座城？你们要我喂养那些兵。我给他们毒药。”

“恶棍！蛆虫！萨拉戈萨的吸血鬼！西班牙人民的耻辱！”我的保护人大喝，攥紧的双拳直逼守财奴那张皱巴巴的脸。“我宁可永堕地狱不得超生，也不愿做你这样的人，哪怕只做一分钟的坎迪奥拉！你这黑了心的良心，你这乖张邪恶的灵魂，全城上下唯独你一个，拒绝把家财献给祖国的爱国军队，你不觉得羞愧吗？只因这桩卑劣行径，人人都恨你入骨，这千夫所指压在你身上，难道不比蒙卡约（Moncayo）满山的岩石一齐塌下来压在你身上还重吗？”

===Original===
“Stop your music and leave me in peace,” said Don Jerónimo, starting to the door.

“Look here, you unclean reptile,” cried Montoria, detaining him, “I have told you that I am not going without the flour. If you do not produce it with good grace, as every good Spaniard does, you shall be made to give it by force. I will pay you forty-eight reales per sack﻿—its price before the siege.”

“Forty-eight reales,” exclaimed Candiola, with an expression of rancor, “I will sell my skin at that price before the flour. I would pay more than that for it. The accursed mob! Shall they be supported by me, Señor de Montoria?”

“You may thank them, miserable usurer, because they have not put an end to your useless life. Does not the generosity of this people surprise you? In the other siege, while we were enduring the greatest privations in order to get money together, your heart of stone remained insensible, and they could not pull out of you one old shirt to cover the nakedness of a poor soldier, or one piece of bread to appease his hunger. Saragossa has not forgotten your infamies. Do you not remember that after the battle of the fourth of August, when the wounded were distributed throughout the city, and two were assigned to you, and rang at your door, it was not possible for them to get their shadows into this wretched door? On the night of the fourth they arrived at your door, and with their weak hands they rang for you to open to them; but their moans and suffering did not move your heart of brass. You came to the door, and kicked them into the street, saying that your house was not a hospital. Unworthy son of Saragossa! but you have not the soul of a son of Saragossa. You were born a Mallorcan, of the blood of a Jew!”

===Chinese===
“收起你那套高调，让我清净清净，”唐·赫罗尼莫说着，抬脚就要往门里走。

“站住，你这龌龊的爬虫，”蒙特里亚喝住他，“我告诉过你，不拿到面粉我不走。你要是不肯像每个好西班牙人那样痛痛快快地交出来，那就强制你交。我按每袋四十八里亚尔付账——这是围城之前的价钱。”

“四十八里亚尔！”坎迪奥拉嚷起来，一脸怨毒，“照这个价钱，我宁可先卖自己这张皮，也轮不到卖面粉；真要把它买回来，我出价比这还高。这帮天杀的乌合之众！难道要我出钱供养他们吗，德·蒙特里亚先生？”

“你倒该谢谢他们，可鄙的放贷鬼：他们没有结果了你这条无用的性命。这方百姓的宽宏大量，难道不叫你吃惊？上一次围城，我们为了凑集钱粮，忍受着最大的匮乏，你那颗石头心却纹丝不动；任谁也没能从你身上抠出一件旧衬衫，去遮一遮穷士兵的赤身，也没抠出一块面包，去填一填他们的饥肠。萨拉戈萨没有忘记你的一桩桩丑行。你记不记得，八月四日之战以后，伤员分派到全城各家，分到你名下两个，他们上门拉铃，却连自己的影子也进不了你那扇倒霉的门？四日夜里，他们到了你的门前，用虚弱无力的手拉铃求你开门；可他们的呻吟和苦楚，打动不了你那颗黄铜铸的心。你走到门口，把他们一脚一个踢到街上，还说你的家不是医院。萨拉戈萨的不肖之子！不，你根本没有萨拉戈萨之子的灵魂。你生来是个马略卡人，流的是犹太人的血！”

===Original===
The eyes of Candiola shot fire. His jaw quivered, and his fingers closed convulsively upon the cudgel in his right hand.

“Yes, you have the blood of a Mallorcan Jew. You are no son of this noble city. Do not the moans of those poor wounded men sound in your bat’s ears? One of them, who was bleeding badly, died on this spot where we are standing. The other managed to creep to the market, where he told of what had happened. Infamous scarecrow! Do you suppose that the people of Saragossa are going to forget the morning of the fifth? Candiola, Candiolilla, give me that flour, and we will close this transaction in peace.”

“Montoria, Montorilla,” replied the other, “my ground and my work will not go to fatten idle vagabonds. Ya! Talk to me of charity and generosity and the needs of the poor soldiers! I have heard enough talk about those wretched sponges who are fed at the public cost. The committee of supplies will have no chance to laugh at me. As if we did not understand all this music about ‘succor of the army.’ Montoria, Montorilla, you have a little dough in your own house, isn’t that true? Good dough can be found in the ovens of every patriot, made of the flour given by the foolish blockheads that the committee of supplies knows. Forty-eight reales! A pretty price! Then, in the accounts which will go to the Captain-General it will be set down as if bought at sixty reales, with a snapper of ‘The Virgin del Pilar would not like to be a Frenchwoman.’ ”

===Chinese===
坎迪奥拉的双眼迸出火来。他的下颌直打颤，右手的指头痉挛般地攥紧了木棍。

“不错，你身上流的就是马略卡犹太人的血。你根本不是这座高贵城市之子。那两个可怜伤员的呻吟，难道没有一直响在你这对蝙蝠耳朵里吗？其中一个血流不止，就死在我们此刻站立的这个地方。另一个拼死爬到了市场，把经过原原本本说了出去。无耻的稻草人！你以为萨拉戈萨人会把五日那个早晨忘掉吗？坎迪奥拉，小坎迪奥拉（Candiolilla），把面粉给我，咱们好和和气气地把这桩买卖了结。”

“蒙特里亚，小蒙特里亚（Montorilla），”对方回敬道，“我的田产、我的血汗，休想拿去喂肥那帮游手好闲的二流子。哼！少跟我讲什么慈悲慷慨、什么穷士兵的急难！那起子吃公家饭的寄生虫，我耳朵里都听出茧子来了。补给议会想看我的笑话，没门儿。这套‘援济军队’的调门，只当我们听不出个中门道似的。蒙特里亚，小蒙特里亚，你自己家里就藏着好面团，是不是？每个爱国者的烤炉里都寻得着好面团，那面粉嘛，都是补给议会知道的那些傻瓜蛋子们捐的。四十八里亚尔！好一个价钱！回头呈给总司令的账上，便写作按六十里亚尔购进，末了再添上一条俏皮的尾巴：‘皮拉尔圣母可不愿意当法国女人。’”

===Original===
When he said this, Don José de Montoria, who was already choking with wrath, lost his stirrups, as the saying is, and powerless to contain his indignation, went straight up to Candiola, apparently to slap his face; but the other had with one strategic glance foreseen the movement, and prepared to repel it. Quickly taking the offensive, he threw himself with a catlike spring upon my protector, grasping his neck with both hands and fastening upon him with his strong and bony fingers, at the same time making ready with his teeth, as if he were about to take between them the entire person of his enemy.

There was a brief struggle in which Montoria strove to free himself from those feline claws which had so suddenly made him their captive; but it could be seen in an instant that the nervous strength of the miser could not hold against the muscular strength of the Aragonese patriot. He shook him off violently. Candiola fell to the ground like a dead man.

We heard the cry of a woman from an upper window, and then the snap of a window-shutter closing. In this dramatic moment I wheeled about anxiously towards Augustine, but he had disappeared.

===Chinese===
他这话一出口，早已气塞胸膛的唐·何塞·德·蒙特里亚便像俗话说的那样“折了马镫”，怒火再也按捺不住，径直朝坎迪奥拉逼过去，看那架势是要掴他的脸；不料对方眼风一扫，早料着了这一手，摆开架势预备招架。他抢先发难，猫儿般一纵身扑向我的保护人，双手扼住他的脖颈，用那双瘦硬有力的指头死死扣住，同时龇出牙齿，仿佛要把仇人的整个身子一并咬进牙缝里去。

两人短暂地扭作一团，蒙特里亚拼命想挣脱那双冷不防将他拿住的猫爪；可转眼便见分晓：守财奴那股子狠劲，敌不过这位阿拉贡爱国者一身筋肉的膂力。他猛力一甩，挣脱开来。坎迪奥拉像死人一般直挺挺栽倒在地。

楼上一扇窗子里传出一个女人的哭喊，接着是百叶窗砰然关拢的声响。在这千钧一发的戏剧性时刻，我焦灼地转身去找奥古斯丁，他却已经无影无踪了。

===Original===
Don José de Montoria, mad with rage, kicked angrily at the prostrate body, stammering thickly in his wrath.

“You dirty pickpocket, enriched with the blood of the poor, you dare to call me a thief, to call the members of the committee of supplies thieves! By a thousand *porras*! I will teach you to respect honest people, and you may be thankful that I do not tear out that miserable tongue of yours and throw it to the dogs.”

All this struck us fairly dumb; but presently we snatched the unlucky Candiola from under the feet of his enemy. His first movement was made as if to jump upon him again, but Montoria had gone into the house, calling:

“Come, boys, we will go into the storehouse and get the flour. Quickly, let us make haste, quickly!”

===Chinese===
唐·何塞·德·蒙特里亚怒极了，朝着那倒地的躯体狠狠踢了几脚，气得结结巴巴、含混不清地骂着。

“你这脏扒手，喝穷人的血发的家，竟敢骂我是贼，骂补给议会的委员们是贼！一千个 *波拉*！我要教教你怎样敬重清白人；我不把你那条贱舌头扯下来扔给狗吃，你就该谢天谢地了！”

这一番情景看得我们个个哑口无言；但大家随即把倒霉的坎迪奥拉从他的对头脚下抢救了出来。他的第一个动作像是要重新扑上去，蒙特里亚却已经进了宅子，一面喊着：

“来，小伙子们，进库房搬面粉去。快，赶紧急些，快！”

===Original===
The great number of people who had congregated in the street prevented old Candiola from entering his own house. The gamins, who had come running from all over the neighborhood, took charge of him themselves. Some pulled him forward, others pushed him backward, tearing his clothing to shreds. Others, taking the offensive from afar, threw great chunks of street mud at him. In the meantime a woman came to meet those of us who had entered the lower floor where the storerooms were. At the first glance I recognized the beautiful Mariquilla, altered and trembling, wavering at every step, without power to stand erect or speak, paralyzed with terror. Her fear was so great that we all pitied her, even Montoria.

“You are the daughter of Señor Candiola,” he said, drawing from his pocket a handful of money, and making a brief reckoning on the wall with a bit of charcoal which he picked up from the floor. “Sixty-eight sacks of flour at forty-eight reales is three thousand two hundred and sixty-four. They are not worth half that, for they seem to me decidedly musty. Take it, child, here is the exact amount.”

Mariquilla Candiola made no movement whatever towards taking the money, and Montoria put it down upon a box, saying﻿—

===Chinese===
街上聚拢来的一大群人，把老坎迪奥拉堵得进不了自家的门。四邻八舍飞跑而来的顽童们索性把他“接管”了过去：有的在前头拽，有的在后头搡，把他的衣裳撕成一条条碎片；另一些远远地发动攻势，抡起大块的街泥朝他掷去。这时候，一个女子迎着我们这几个进到底楼库房来的人走了过来。我一眼便认出那是美丽的玛丽基亚——她容色大变，浑身发抖，一步一晃，站也站不直，话也说不出，吓得整个人都瘫软了。她的惊恐实在可怜，我们人人都动了恻隐之心，连蒙特里亚也不例外。

“您是坎迪奥拉先生的女儿吧，”他说着，从口袋里掏出一把钱，又从地上拾起一截炭条，在墙上三下五除二核了一笔账。“六十八袋面粉，每袋四十八里亚尔，合三千二百六十四里亚尔。其实连一半也不值——依我看，这些面粉分明是发过霉的。拿着吧，孩子，这是分毫不差的数目。”

玛丽基亚·坎迪奥拉丝毫没有伸手接钱的意思，蒙特里亚便把钱放在一只木箱上，说——

===Original===
“There it is!”

Then the girl with a brusque and energetic movement which seemed, as it certainly was, the inspiration of her offended dignity, took the money, gold, silver, and copper, and threw it as if it were so many stones into the face of Montoria. The money was scattered all over the floor, and rolled out of the door without much promise of anyone’s finding it all in the future. Immediately afterwards the señorita went without a word into the street. She beheld her father jammed into the crowd; and presently, aided by some young men, unable to see with indifference a woman in distress, she freed him from the infamous captivity in which the boys held him.

The father and daughter entered by the garden gate, as we were beginning to remove the flour.

===Chinese===
“钱在这儿！”

这时，姑娘猛地做了一个决绝而有力的动作——看得出，那确实是被冒犯的尊严激发出来的一股意气——她一把抓起那笔钱，金的银的铜的，当作一块块石子，劈面向蒙特里亚掷去。钱币撒了满地，滚出门外，往后谁也别想把它们一枚枚找齐了。随后，那位小姐一言不发，径自走到街上。她望见父亲被人群死死裹住；不多时，几个不忍见落难女子受窘的年轻人出手相助，帮她把父亲从顽童们那场不光彩的囚困里解救了出来。

父女俩从园门进去了，这时我们已开始搬运面粉。

## XIII / 第十三章

===Original===
When we had finished carrying out the flour, I went and looked for Augustine; but I could find him nowhere, neither in his father’s house, nor at the headquarters of supplies, nor in the Coso, nor in Santa Engracia. At nightfall I found him in the powder mill near San Juan de los Panetes. I have forgotten to say that the Saragossans had improvised a work shop where were turned out daily nine or ten quintals of powder. I saw Augustine helping the workmen put into sacks and barrels the powder made during the day. He was working with feverish activity.

“Do you see this enormous heap of powder?” he said to me when I approached him. “Do you see those sacks and those barrels all full of the same material? Well, Gabriel, it seems to me very little.”

“I don’t know what you are trying to say.”

===Chinese===
我们把那批面粉搬运完毕之后，我便去找奥古斯丁；可哪儿也找不见他——他父亲的宅子里没有，补给总部没有，科索大街没有，圣恩格拉西亚也没有。挨到暮色四合，我才在圣胡安-德洛斯帕内塔斯近旁的火药磨坊（powder mill）里找到他。我忘了交代：萨拉戈萨人曾临时赶起一座工场，每天能制出九到十公担（quintal）火药。我看见奥古斯丁正帮着工人们把当日制成的火药装进麻袋和木桶。他干得狂热起劲。

我走近他时，他对我说道：“你瞧这一大堆火药？你瞧那些麻袋、那些木桶，装的全是同样的东西？可是加夫列尔，在我看来还是太少了。”

“我不明白你想说什么。”

===Original===
“I say that if this immense quantity of powder were as big as Saragossa I should like it still better. Yes, and in such a case I should like to be the only inhabitant of this great city. What a pleasure! Listen, Gabriel! If it were so, I would myself set fire to it, and fly into the clouds, torn to pieces in the horrible explosion like pieces of rock which a volcano throws to a distance of a hundred leagues. I would be hurled to the fifth heaven, and of my members, scattered everywhere, there would be no memory! Death, Gabriel, death is what I desire! But I desire a death﻿—I do not explain it to you. My desperation is so great that to die of a gunshot or a sabre-thrust would not satisfy me. I long to be rent asunder, and diffused through space in a thousand burning particles. I pant to feel myself in the bosom of a flame-bearing cloud. My spirit yearns, if only for an infinitesimal instant, the delight of seeing this wretched body reduced to powder. Gabriel, I am desperate. Do you see this powder? Imagine within my breast all the flames which this could make. Did you see her when she went out to get her father? Did you see her when she threw the money? I was in a corner where I could see it all. Mariquilla does not know that that man who maltreated her father is my own! Did you see how the boys threw mud at poor Candiola? I realize that Candiola is a wretch. But she, what fault has she? She and I, what fault have we? None, Gabriel. My heart is broken, and thirsts for a thousand deaths. I cannot live. I will run into the place of greatest danger and fling myself into the fire of the French. After what I have seen today, I and the earth on which I dwell may not be together.”

===Chinese===
“我是说，倘若这一大堆火药能有萨拉戈萨城那么大，我倒更要称心几分。是啊，真到那个地步，我就巴望做这座大城里唯一的居民了。那才叫快意！你听着，加夫列尔！果真那样，我就亲手点火烧了它，在那骇人的爆炸中粉身碎骨、飞进云霄，就像火山把一块块岩石抛到百里格开外。我会被掷上第五重天，我的肢体撒遍四方，从此无人记得！死，加夫列尔，我渴求的就是死！可我渴求的那种死——我跟你说不明白。我绝望到了这步田地，一枪毙命、一刀洞穿，都满足不了我。我渴望被撕成碎片，化作千万粒燃烧的微尘弥散到太空里去。我喘息着渴望感到自己置身于一座驮着烈焰的云的怀抱。哪怕只有无穷小的一瞬，我的灵魂也渴念着要看一看这具可悲的躯壳化为齑粉的快意。加夫列尔，我绝望透了。你瞧见这火药了？想象一下它所能生出的全部烈焰，都装在我的胸膛里。她出去接她父亲的时候，你瞧见了吗？她把钱掷出去的时候，你瞧见了吗？我当时正缩在一个角落里，什么都瞧在眼里。玛丽基亚不知道，那个虐待她父亲的人，正是我的父亲！你瞧见那帮顽童朝可怜的坎迪奥拉扔泥巴了吗？我知道坎迪奥拉是个恶棍。可她有什么罪？她和我，我们有什么罪？没有，加夫列尔。我的心已经碎了，一千个死也解不了渴。我活不下去了。我要奔到最危险的地方去，一头扎进法国人的炮火里。今天亲眼见了这一切之后，我与我栖身的这片大地，再不能相容了。”

===Original===
I drew him away from the place, taking him to the walls; and we went to work on the fortifications which were being made in Las Tenerías, the weakest point in the city since the destruction of San José and Santa Engracia. I have already said that from the mouth of the Huerva to San José stretched a line of fifty mouths of fire. Against this formidable line of attack what avail was our fortified circuit?

The quarter of Las Tenerías extended from the eastern part of the city, between the Huerva and the old part of the town, perfectly outlined yet by the wide road which is called the Coso. It was at the beginning of this century a village of mean houses, almost all inhabited by laborers and artisans, and the religious houses there had none of the splendor of the monuments of Saragossa. The general plan of this district is approximately the segment of a circle whose arc curves out to the open country, and whose chord unites it to the city, from the Puerta Quemada to the rise at the Sepulcro.

From that line to the circumference ran several streets, some of them broken, like the Calles de la Diezma, Barrio Verde, de los Clavos, and de Pabostre. Some of these were marked not by rows of houses, but by walls, and lacking sometimes one thing and sometimes another. The streets spread out into formless little squares or yards or barren gardens. I describe badly because in the days I refer to the heaps of ruins left by the first siege had been used to mount batteries and raise barricades in points where the houses did not offer a natural defence.

===Chinese===
我把他从那地方拉走，带他到城墙边去；我们随即投入了特内里亚斯的筑城劳作——自从圣何塞与圣恩格拉西亚被毁之后，那里成了全城最薄弱的一点。我已经说过，从韦尔瓦河口到圣何塞，横亘着一条五十个喷火炮口的战线。面对这条威势汹汹的攻击线，我们那圈环城工事又济得什么事？

特内里亚斯区坐落在城东，夹在韦尔瓦河与老城之间，至今仍被那条唤作科索的宽阔大道清清楚楚地勾出轮廓。本世纪初叶，这里还是一片寒酸屋舍凑成的村落，居民几乎全是劳工与匠人；区里的修道院房舍，也没有半点萨拉戈萨煌煌建筑群的气象。全区的总体格局近似一块弓形地带：弓背向外拱向旷野，弓弦则把它连上城区——从克马达门直到圣墓（Sepulcro）那一带的坡岗。

从那条弓弦到圆周之间，穿行着好几条街巷，有的已破断残缺，如迭斯马街（Calle de la Diezma）、巴里奥贝尔德（Barrio Verde）、克拉沃斯街（Calle de los Clavos）和帕博斯特雷街（Calle de Pabostre）。其中有些地段，界定街道的不是成行的房屋，而是一堵堵院墙，缺这少那，不成体统。街道漫散开去，汇成一块块不成形状的小方场、院落或荒园。我描写得很拙劣，因为在我所说的那些日子里，第一次围城留下的一堆堆废墟，早已被用来设置炮位、垒筑街垒，填在那些房屋不足以天然御敌的地段上了。

===Original===
Near the fortification of the Ebro were some remnants of an ancient wall, with various little towers of masonry which some persons supposed to be from the hands of the Romans, and others judged to be the work of the Moors. In my time﻿—I do not know how it may be now﻿—these pieces of wall seemed to be mortised into the houses, or rather the houses were mortised into them, appearing like props and corners of that ancient work, blackened but not crumbled by the passing of so many centuries. The new had been built in a confused way upon the ruins of the old, as the Spanish people had developed and grown upon the spoils of other peoples of mixed bloods, until they became as they are today.

The general aspect of the district of Las Tenerías brought to the imagination pleasant fancies of all that had taken place during Moorish rule, the abundance of brick, the long gable ends, the irregular fronts, the window lattices with shutters, the complete architectural anarchy, making it impossible to know where one house ended and another began, or of distinguishing whether this had two floors or three, or if that roof served to support the walls of that one over there. The streets at best ended in yards with no ways out. The archways, which gave entrance to a little square, reminded me that here was a vista upon another Spanish people, very different from those now here.

This amalgamation of houses which I have described to you, this suburb built up by many generations of laborers and peasants and tanners, according to the caprice of each, without order or harmony, had prepared itself for the defence on the twenty-fourth and twenty-fifth days of January, at the time when the French began to display their pomp of attack by placing forces on that side. All the families living in the houses of this suburb proceeded to build works according to their own strategic instincts. There were military engineers in petticoats who demonstrated a profound knowledge of war by walling up certain spaces and opening others to the light, and for purposes of firing. The walls of the eastern side were spiked along their length. The turrets of the wall of Caesar Augustus, built to resist arrows and sling stones, now upheld cannon.

===Chinese===
埃布罗河工事近旁，残存着一段古墙，连着几座砖石砌的小塔楼；有人推想出自罗马人之手，也有人断为摩尔人的营造。在我那个年代——如今如何，我不得而知——这几截墙体像是榫接进了两侧的房屋，或者不如说，是房屋榫接进了墙体，看上去恰似那古老工程的支木与墙角，历经数百年而熏黑未酥。新的东西杂乱无章地压盖在旧的废墟上营造起来，正如西班牙这个民族在其他混血诸族的劫余之上发育滋长，终至今天的模样。

特内里亚斯区的总貌，引人悠然想见摩尔人治下的一切旧事：满目砖房，长长的山墙端壁，歪斜不整的临街立面，带木窗板的格子窗，十足的建筑无政府状态——一幢房子在哪里收尾、另一幢从哪里起头，无从分辨；也辨不清这一幢是两层还是三层，那一片屋顶是不是在替远处那一堵墙受力。街道走到尽头，至多通进一座无路可出的院子。那些通向小方场的拱门令我想起：此地曾展现出另一个西班牙民族的图景，与如今居于此地的民族，已判然两样。

我向你们描绘的这片犬牙交错的屋舍，这片由一代又一代劳工、农夫和制革匠各凭一时兴致搭盖起来、毫无章法与谐调的城郊，在一月二十四日、二十五日——正当法军把兵力压向这一侧、开始摆开它堂堂攻势的时候——已把自己整备停当，投入防御。住在这片城郊屋舍里的人家，全都照着各自的战略本能动手修筑工事。这里还有穿裙子的军事工程师，她们封死某些口子，又为采光和开火另辟一些洞口，显出精深的兵法学识。东侧的墙体沿全线插满了尖桩。凯撒·奥古斯都（Caesar Augustus）的城墙上，那些当年为抵御箭矢与投石而筑的小塔楼，如今扛起的是大炮。

===Original===
If any one of these pieces were turned upon one of the neighboring roofs, the roof or the entire house, whatever was there, would be immediately blown to pieces. Many passages had been obstructed, and two of the religious edifices of the suburb, San Augustine and Las Mónicas, were veritable fortresses. The wall had been rebuilt and strengthened; the batteries had been joined together, and our engineers had calculated the positions and the reach of the enemy’s guns very well, in order to accommodate our defences to them.

Our line had two advanced points, the mill of Goicoechea and a house which, because it belonged to a certain Don Victoriano González, has gone into history by the name of the Casa de González. This line, running from the Puerta Quemada, met first the battery of Palafox, then the Molino, the mill, in the city, then the garden of San Augustine; it continued to the mill of Goicoechea, situated a little out of the district, then to the orchard of Las Mónicas, and on to those of San Augustine; further up, a great battery and the house of González. This is all that I remember of Las Tenerías. There was over there a place called the Sepulcro, because of its nearness to a church of that name. More than one portion of the suburb, indeed, deserved the name of sepulchre. I tell you no more in order not to tire you with these descriptive minutiae, unnecessary to one who knows those glorious places, and insufficient for one who has been unable to visit them.

===Chinese===
这些炮位里只要有一门调转炮口，对准左近哪片屋顶，那屋顶、或整幢房子连同其中的一切，顷刻便会被轰得粉碎。许多通道已经堵死，区内两座宗教建筑——圣奥古斯丁（San Augustine）与拉斯莫尼卡斯（Las Mónicas）——俨然是两座真正的堡垒。城墙重新砌起、加固了；各座炮台连成了一气，我们的工程师把敌炮的位置与射程估算得头头是道，好叫我们的防御处处对着它们布设。

我方防线有两个前出据点：一是戈伊科切亚磨坊（mill of Goicoechea），二是一座宅子——因宅主是一位唐·维克托里亚诺·冈萨雷斯（Don Victoriano González），它便以“冈萨雷斯之家”（Casa de González）之名载入了史册。这条防线自克马达门起，先经帕拉福克斯炮台，接着是城内的莫利诺（Molino）磨坊，再到圣奥古斯丁的园子；续而延至略出区界的戈伊科切亚磨坊，接拉斯莫尼卡斯的果园，再接圣奥古斯丁的果园；再往上，是一座大炮台和冈萨雷斯之家。关于特内里亚斯，我记得的就这么些了。那边还有一处地方唤作“圣墓”，因毗邻一座同名教堂而得名。说实在的，这城郊不止一处地方，当得起“坟墓”这个名目。我不再多讲了，免得这些描摹的细枝末节把你们讲乏了——对认得那些光荣之地的人，这些用不着；对无缘踏访的人，这些又不够。

## XIV / 第十四章

===Original===
Augustine Montoria and I stood guard with our battalion in the Molino until after nightfall, the hour when we were relieved by the Huesca volunteers; then we permitted ourselves to be all night outside the lines. But it must not be believed that during these hours we strolled about hand in hand; for when our military services were over, there were others no less onerous in the interior of the city, where the wounded had already been carried to La Seo and to the Pilar﻿—burning houses to carry things out of, or materials to carry to the friars, the canons, and the civil officials, who were making cartridges in San Juan de los Panetes.
Montoria and I went there by way of the Calle de Pabostre. I walked along munching a crust of bread with good appetite. My companion, taciturn and gloomy, amused himself by throwing his to the dogs that we met as we walked along. Although I exerted my imagination in efforts to cheer his sad spirit, he remained dull and insensible to it all, replying but sadly to my merry chatter. As we entered the Coso, he said to me﻿—
“It is ten by the clock of the Torre Nueva. Do you know﻿—I wish to go there tonight.”

===Chinese===
奥古斯丁·蒙特里亚和我随本营在莫利诺磨坊一直值守到入夜以后，直到韦斯卡义勇军前来换岗；换岗之后，这一夜我们便由着自己待在了防线之外。可千万别以为这几个钟头我们是手拉着手闲逛过去的；军务一了，城内还有别的差事等着，一样吃重：伤员们早已被抬进了塞奥和皮拉尔圣母教堂——有失火的房子，得从里头往外抢搬东西；有一包包材料，要送给修士、座堂神父和市政官员们，他们正在圣胡安-德洛斯帕内塔斯赶制弹药筒。
我和奥古斯丁取道帕博斯特雷街（Calle de Pabostre）往那里去。我一路嚼着一块硬面包皮，吃得津津有味。我的同伴却沉默寡言、郁郁寡欢，只顾把他自己那份抛给沿途遇见的狗，权当消遣。尽管我绞尽脑汁想逗他愁苦的心怀展颜，他却始终木然，对这一切无动于衷，只恹恹地应付着我的说笑。走进科索大街时，他对我说——
“新塔的钟敲十点了。你知道吗——我今夜想上那边去一趟。”

===Original===
“Tonight you will not be able to go. Try to stifle the flame of love in its ashes, while we are threatened by those other burning hearts, the flaming bombs which are coming to break in the houses and among the people.”
It was even so. The bombarding, which had not ceased during all the day, was continued during the night, though with a little less vigor; and from time to time projectiles fell, augmenting the already large number of victims within the city.
“I must go there this night,” he said. “Did not Mariquilla see me among all those who crowded in front of the door of her house? Will she not think me one of those who abused her father?”

===Chinese===
“今夜你可去不成。想法子把爱火的焰苗捂灭在灰烬里吧——眼下威胁着咱们的，是另外几颗燃烧的心：那些烈焰腾腾、眼看就要砸进住家、砸进人堆里的炸弹。”
事实正是如此。炮击一整天未尝间断，入夜后仍在继续，只是火力稍减；炮弹不时落下来，给城里本已数目可观的死难者再添新鬼。
“今夜我非去不可，”他说，“挤在她家门前的那群人里，玛丽基亚难道没有瞧见我吗？她会不会把我当作糟践她父亲的那些人中的一个？”

===Original===
“I don’t believe so. That young woman would know how to distinguish between individuals. She has already made inquiries, and now is no time for stolen sweets. Do you see? From that house coming this way are some poor women in need of help. Look, one of them is not able to creep further, and falls to the ground. Is it not possible that the Señorita Doña Mariquilla Candiola has also gone to care for the wounded at San Pablo or the Pilar?”
“I do not believe so.”
“Or perhaps where they are making cartridges?”
“I believe that still less. She would be in her house, and there is where I wish to go, Gabriel. You may go and see to the carrying of the wounded, or to the powder, or whatever you please, but I am going there!”
As he said this, Pirli presented himself to us in his friar’s habit, already torn and hanging in a thousand fragments, and on his head the French engineer’s helmet, badly battered, but plated and plumed, and making our hero look less like a soldier than a carnival figure.

===Chinese===
“我看不会。那位姑娘分得清张三李四。她已经打听过动静了，再说如今也不是偷尝甜头的时候。你瞧见了吗？从那所房子这边，来了几个需要人搭把手的不幸妇人。看，其中一个再也挪不动了，倒在了地上。堂娜·玛丽基亚·坎迪奥拉（Doña Mariquilla Candiola）小姐，会不会也上圣巴勃罗或者皮拉尔圣母教堂照看伤员去了？”
“我看不会去。”
“要么，是到制造弹药筒的地方去了？”
“那更不会。她准是待在自己家里，而我恰恰就是要到那儿去，加夫列尔。你尽可以去看抬伤员、去看火药，或是随你高兴干什么去，我可要到那儿去了！”
话音未落，皮尔利就到了我们跟前。他还穿着那身修士袍，已经扯得破碎不堪、挂成千百条布缕；头上扣着那顶法国工兵的钢盔，磕碰得七歪八凹，却仍旧镀光闪亮、翎毛高翘，叫我们这位好汉瞧着不像个兵，倒更像狂欢节里的人物。

===Original===
“Are you coming to help carry the wounded?” he asked. “They have just killed two more for us that we are carrying to San Pablo. They need men there to open the ditch where they are burying our dead of yesterday, but I have worked enough. I am going to the house of Manuela Sancho to see if I can get a snatch of sleep. But, first, we are going to dance a little. Don’t you want to come along?”
“No, we are going to San Pablo,” I replied, “to bury the dead. There is enough to do.”
“They say that so many dead make the air bad, and that is why there are so many ill of the fever. That is finishing them faster than their wounds, over by the other barricade. I would rather have some ‘hot cakes’ than the epidemic. A ‘señora’ wouldn’t scare me, but a chill and a fever would. So then you are going to bury the dead?”
“Yes,” said Augustine, “let us bury the dead.”

===Chinese===
“你们是来帮忙抬伤员的吗？”他问道。“刚刚又给我们打死了两个，正要抬到圣巴勃罗去呢。那边缺人手掘坑，昨天阵亡的弟兄们要葬进去，可我可是干够了。我要到玛努埃拉·桑乔家去，看看能不能抢个空儿打个盹。不过先不忙这个，咱们先去跳一会儿舞。你们不想一块儿去吗？”
“不去，我们要到圣巴勃罗去，”我答道，“去安葬死者。有的是活儿要干。”
“人家说死人一多，空气就坏了，所以发寒热的人才这么多。在另一头的街垒那边，时疫送人上路比伤口还快。我宁可挨几发‘热煎饼’，也不愿染上时疫。一颗‘太太’吓不倒我，一场寒热病可就吓倒我了。这么说，你们是要去埋死人了？”
“对，”奥古斯丁说，“咱们去安葬死者吧。”

===Original===
“In San Pablo there are no less than forty wounded,” answered Pirli; “and, at the rate we’re going there, we’ll soon be more dead than living. Don’t you want a little diversion? If you are not going to work on the ditch, why not come along to the cartridge factory? All the girls will be there, and from time to time they will give us some singing, or cheer our souls with a little dancing.”
“We have no fault to find with all that. Will Manuela Sancho be there too?”
“No, the girls there are the young ladies of Saragossa, the señoritas who have been called into service by the committee of safety. There are a great many of them in the hospitals too. They invite themselves for that service. And it would be a queer one who would use her eyes so little as not to make a match for herself, if not for this year, then for next!”

===Chinese===
“圣巴勃罗的伤员少说也有四十个，”皮尔利答道，“照那边这个死法下去，咱们很快就要死的多过活的了。你们不想解个闷儿吗？既然不去掘坑，何不一块儿上弹药筒工场去？姑娘们全在那儿，隔三差五就给我们唱上一曲，或是跳上一小段舞，给咱们的灵魂鼓鼓劲儿。”
“这些我们都巴不得呢。玛努埃拉·桑乔也会在那儿吗？”
“不在。那边的姑娘们是萨拉戈萨城里的小姐们，是被治安委员会（committee of safety）征去服役的各位小姐。医院里也有许许多多这样的人。她们都是自告奋勇去尽这份劳的。哪个姑娘要是眼皮子这么不济，连自己的一门亲事都相不成——今年不成，明年也该成了——那才叫怪人呢！”

===Original===
We heard the rushing sound of many footsteps behind us, and, turning, we saw a great number of people, among whose voices we recognized that of Don José de Montoria. He was very angry at seeing us there, and exclaimed﻿—
“What are you doing here, idiots? Three strong hearty men standing here with their hands folded, when there is such a lack of men for the work to be done! Go along with you! Clear out of here! March, you little tin soldiers! Do you see those two posts there on the Trenque knoll with beams crossed on top from which six ropes are hanging? Do you see that gallows set up in that place for traitors? Well, it’s for loafers, too. Get along to work, or I’ll show your carcasses how to move with my fists.”
We followed him until we came quite near the gallows, where the six ropes were swaying commandingly in the wind, ready to strangle traitors or cowards. Montoria seized his son by the arm, and pointed to the horrible apparatus with an energetic gesture, saying﻿—

===Chinese===
我们听见身后传来一阵杂沓急促的脚步声，回头一看，只见一大群人涌来；那片嘈杂的人声里，我们辨出了唐·何塞·德·蒙特里亚的嗓音。他见我们站在那里，勃然大怒，喝道——
“你们在这儿干什么，蠢货们？干活的人手这么紧缺，三个壮实硬朗的汉子倒背着手闲站在这儿！都给我走！滚开！开步走，你们这些小锡兵！瞧见特伦克（Trenque）土丘上那两根柱子没有？柱顶上架着交叉的木梁，梁上垂下六根绳子。瞧见那座专为叛徒们竖起的绞刑架没有？哼，它也是给懒汉们预备的。快去干活，不然我这双拳头就要教教你们这身臭皮囊该怎么挪窝。”
我们跟着他走，一直来到离绞刑架很近的地方；那六根绳索正在风里威风凛凛地摆荡，随时预备着勒死叛徒或懦夫。蒙特里亚一把攥住儿子的胳膊，用一个斩钉截铁的手势直指那副可怕的刑具，说道——

===Original===
“Here you can see what we have been getting ready this evening. Look! There’s where those who do not do their duty will be entertained. On with you! I who am old never get tired, but you young healthy men act as if you were made of putty. The invincible men of the first siege have almost all worked themselves to death; and we old men, sirs, are obliged to set an example to these dandies who if they miss dining for a week begin to complain and beg for broth. I would give you broth of powder, and soup of cannon balls, you cowards! Go, and see that you help to bury the dead and carry ammunition to the walls.”
“And assist at the hell which this damned epidemic is spreading,” said one of those who had accompanied Montoria.
“I don’t know what to think of this thing which the doctors call the epidemic,” answered Don José. “I call it fear, sirs, pure fear. They take a chill; then they have spasms and a fever; then they turn green, and they die. What is all that but the effect of fear? Our strong men all seem to be gone, yes, señors. Ah, what men those were in the first siege! Now when the soldiers have been firing and been fired at for a trifle of ten hours, they begin to fall down with fatigue, and say they can do no more. There’s one man who had lost only a leg and a half who began screaming and calling upon all the holy martyrs, begging that they put him to bed. Nothing but cowardice, pure cowardice! Today several soldiers left Palafox’s battery who had a good sound arm apiece left to fight with. And they began to beg for broth! They had better drink their own blood, which is the best broth in the world. I say the race of men of courage is finished and done with, *porra*! a thousand *porras*!”

===Chinese===
“瞧见了吧，这就是我们今晚备下的名堂。看！不尽本分的人，就在那儿领招待。开步走！我这把年纪的人从来不叫累，你们这些年富力强的，倒像是油灰捏的。第一次围城里那些无敌的汉子，几乎个个都把自己活活累死在了工地上；我们这些老头子，诸位，还得给这帮花花公子作个榜样——他们但凡一个礼拜没吃上正餐，就嚷嚷叫苦、讨肉汤喝了。我倒要请你们这帮懦夫喝火药熬的汤、吃炮弹煮的粥！去，好好帮着安葬死者，把弹药搬上城墙去。”
“还得去照应那被该死的时疫四处播撒开来的地狱，”随蒙特里亚同来的一个人说。
“医生们称作时疫的这个玩意儿，我真不知道该怎么看，”唐·何塞答道，“依我说，这是害怕，诸位，纯粹的害怕。先打一个寒战；跟着抽搐、发烧；再往后脸色发青，人就死了。这一切，除了是害怕闹出来的效验，还能是什么？我们的壮士好像全都绝了种，可不是嘛，先生们。啊，第一次围城那会儿的，都是些什么样的人！如今当兵的才打了区区十个钟头的枪、挨了十个钟头的炮，就开始累趴下，说再也顶不住了。有那么一位，统共丢了条半腿，就嚎叫起来，把诸位圣殉道者求告了个遍，只求人家放他上床。除了怯懦，纯粹的怯懦，什么也不是！今天帕拉福克斯炮台上有几个兵撤了下来，他们每人分明都还留着一条结结实实的好胳膊可以打仗呢。居然还张口讨肉汤喝！他们顶好去喝自己的血，那才是天底下最好的肉汤。要我说，有胆量的人类是完蛋了、绝根了，*波拉*！一千个*波拉*！”

===Original===
“Tomorrow the French will attack Las Tenerías,” said the other. “If, as a result, there are many wounded, I don’t see where we are going to put them.”
“Wounded!” exclaimed Montoria. “We don’t wish to see any wounded here. The dead do not hinder us. We can pile them up in a heap; but the wounded﻿—ugh! Our soldiers are no longer fearless, and I’ll wager that those who are defending the best positions will not risk seeing themselves decimated; they will abandon them as soon as they see a couple of dozen French heads above each rampart. What feebleness! After all, ’twill be as God wills, and as for the wounded and sick, we will take care of them. Why not? Have you taken many fowls today?”
“Several dozens, of which more than half were given, and for the rest we paid six reales and a half. A few were not willing to give.”

===Chinese===
“明天法国人就要进攻特内里亚斯了，”那人说，“到时候要是伤员成堆，我真看不出往哪儿安置他们。”
“伤员！”蒙特里亚嚷道，“我们才不愿在这儿见到什么伤员。死人碍不着我们的事，码成堆就是了；可伤员——呸！咱们的士兵再也不是天不怕地不怕的了，我敢打赌，守着最好阵地的那些人，绝不肯眼睁睁看着自己十停折去一停；每段壁垒上一冒出两打法国人的脑袋，他们立马就会弃阵而走。多么不中用！说到底，一切都凭天主意旨；至于伤员病号嘛，我们自会照看他们。这有什么不成？今天你们收来多少只鸡了？”
“收了几十来只，一多半是人家自愿献出的，其余的我们付了六个半里亚尔。有几户不肯给。”

===Original===
“All right. To think that a man like me should occupy himself with fowls in days like these! What’s that you say? Some were not willing to give? The Captain-General authorized me to impose fines upon those who do not contribute to the defence. We will just gently get the law on those milksops and traitors. Hark, señors! A bomb fell then in the neighborhood of the Torre Nueva. Did you see it? Did you hear it? What a horrible explosion! I’ll wager that it is Divine Providence more than the French batteries that have sent it against the house of that petrified, soulless Jew who looks on with indifference and contempt at his neighbors’ distress. People are running that way. It seems that the house is on fire, or falling down. No, don’t you run, you miserable fellows. Let it burn, let it fall to the earth in a thousand pieces. It is the house of the miser Candiola, who would not give one peseta to save the whole human race from a new deluge. Eh, where are you going? You are going to run there too? No, come along. Follow me! We can be of more use elsewhere.”
We were going in a crowd to the Orphanage. Augustine, impelled no doubt by the beating of his heart, suddenly started as if to direct his steps towards the Plazuela San Felipe, following the great crowd hastening towards that place. But detained forcibly by his father he continued, though with bad grace, in our company. Something was certainly burning near the Torre Nueva, and on the tower the precious arabesques and bricks shone redly, because of the nearness of the fire. That graceful leaning column could be distinguished, crimson in the black night, and at the same time from its huge belfry a great lamentation fell upon the air.

===Chinese===
“这就对了。想想看，像我这样一个人，竟在这种年月里操起收鸡的心来！你说什么？有几户不肯给？总司令授了权给我，凡不肯为守城出力的，都由我处以罚金。咱们就好生拿王法治一治那些软骨头和叛徒。听，先生们！方才一颗炸弹落在新塔左近了。瞧见没有？听见没有？好可怕的一声炸响！我敢打赌，把这颗炸弹打发到那座房子上去的，与其说是法国人的炮台，不如说是神圣的天意——那房子的主人，是个铁石心肠、没有灵魂的犹太人，眼看左邻右舍遭殃，只在一旁冷眼旁观、嗤之以鼻。人们正朝那边跑。瞧那样子，房子是着了火，要不就是塌了。不，你们别跑，你们这些可怜虫。让它烧，让它摔个粉身碎骨。那是吝啬鬼坎迪奥拉的房子——哪怕出一个比塞塔就能救全人类躲过一场新的大洪水，他也是不肯的。喂，你要上哪儿去？你也想往那边跑？不行，来，跟我走！别处更有用得着我们的地方。”
我们一大群人正朝孤儿院方向走去。奥古斯丁无疑是心头的搏动在驱使，猛然一个侧身，仿佛要抬脚奔圣费利佩小广场（Plazuela San Felipe）去，去追那股匆匆赶往那边的大人流；却被他父亲强行拽住，只得老大不情愿地依旧与我们同行。新塔左近确实有什么东西烧着了，火头就在近旁，塔上那些珍贵的阿拉伯式花纹和砖石映得通红。黑夜里，那座秀美倾侧的塔柱红艳艳地矗立着，轮廓分明；与此同时，一阵浩大的哀声从它那巨大的钟楼洒向夜空。

===Original===
We reached San Pablo.
“Go on, boys, loungers! Help those who are opening the ditch. It must be wide and deep. It is a garment wherein they will enrobe forty bodies.”
We began upon the work, digging earth from the ditch which was being opened in the court of the church. Augustine was digging with me, but at every instant he turned his eyes in the direction of the Torre Nueva.

===Chinese===
我们到了圣巴勃罗。
“干起来，小伙子们，闲汉们！去帮那些掘坑的人一把。坑得又宽又深。那是一袭衣衾，要裹殓四十具尸身哩。”
我们动手干起来，在教堂的庭院里掘那条正在开挖的壕坑。奥古斯丁同我一起掘土，可他时时刻刻都把眼睛转向新塔的方向。

===Original===
“It is a terrible fire. It seems as if it is going down a little, Gabriel. I long to throw myself into this grave which we are opening.”
“Don’t be in a hurry,” I answered him. “Perhaps tomorrow will throw us into it without our asking. This is no time for foolishness; it is time to work.”
“Do you not see? I believe that the fire is extinguished.”
“Yes, the whole house has probably burned down. Candiola was sure to be shut up in his cellar with his money, and the fire couldn’t reach him. Don’t worry.”

===Chinese===
“这场火真吓人。不过火势像是下去了一点，加夫列尔。我恨不得一头栽进咱们正挖着的这座坟里。”
“别急，”我答道，“说不定明天不必咱们开口，自有人把咱们扔进去。现在不是犯傻的时候，是干活的时候。”
“你没瞧见吗？我看火已经熄了。”
“是啊，整座房子多半烧塌了。坎迪奥拉准是揣着他的钱关在地窖里，火烧不着他。放心吧。”

===Original===
“Gabriel, I must go there, if only for a moment. I wish to see if the fire was really in his house. If my father returns, tell him that I will be back in a second.”
The sudden appearance of Don José de Montoria prevented Augustine making the flight which he had just planned, and we two continued digging in the great sepulchre. They began to bring out bodies; and the sick and wounded, who were constantly being brought from without, saw, as they were taken into the church, the wide bed which we were preparing for them. At last the ditch was sufficiently deep, and we were ordered to cease digging. The work went on, and corpses were brought, one by one, and cast into the great sepulchre, while clergymen and pious women upon their knees repeated the mournful words of the service. There was room enough for all, and nothing remained to be done for them but to cover them with earth. Don José Montoria, with head uncovered, reciting in a low voice a paternoster, threw the first handful. Then our shovels and spades began with all speed to cover them. Our work ended, we all knelt down, and prayed in hushed tones. Augustine Montoria said to me when this was done﻿—
“We will go now. My father will march himself off. Go and tell him that we are going to relieve two friends on duty who have a sick one in their family and wish us to see him. Tell him, for God’s sake! I haven’t the courage, then in an instant we can be there.”

===Chinese===
“加夫列尔，我必须到那边去一趟，哪怕就一小会儿。我要看看起火的到底是不是他家。要是我父亲回来了，告诉他我一转眼就回来。”
唐·何塞·德·蒙特里亚的突然出现，破灭了奥古斯丁刚刚筹划好的这场脱逃，我们两个便继续在那座巨大的墓穴里掘土。人们开始把尸首一具具抬运过来；而那些不断从外面抬来的病人和伤员，在被抬进教堂时，都望见了我们正为他们铺备的那张宽床。壕坑终于够深了，上头传令停止挖掘。工程继续进行，尸体一具接一具抬来，抛进那座巨大的墓穴；教士们和虔敬的妇人们跪在一旁，一遍遍念着安葬经文中哀伤的词句。地方尽够容纳所有人，剩下要为他们做的，不过是覆上一抔黄土。唐·何塞·蒙特里亚光着头，低声诵着一段《天主经》，撒下了第一把土。随后，我们的铁锹铁铲飞速动起来，把坟土覆上。活计完毕，我们全体跪下，低声祈祷。事毕，奥古斯丁·蒙特里亚对我说——
“咱们这就走。我父亲自己会开拔的。你去告诉他，说咱们去替两个当值的朋友换班，他们家里有个病人，想叫咱们去看看他。看在上帝份上，替我告诉他！这话我自己没胆子说。这样一来，咱们一眨眼就能到那边了。”

## XV / 第十五章

===Original===
We deceived the old man and went. The night was now far advanced, as the interment which I have just described had lasted more than three hours. The light of the fire could no longer be seen. The mass of the tower was lost in the darkness of night, and its great bell did not sound except now and then to announce the coming of a bomb. We arrived soon at the Plazuela of San Felipe. Seeing the roof of a house near the church still smoking, we knew that it was this, and not the house of Candiola, which three hours before the flames had attacked.

“God has preserved it!” cried Augustine, joyously. “If the meanness of her father should bring divine anger upon that roof, the virtues and innocence of Mariquilla would preserve it! Let us go there.”

In the Plazuela of San Felipe there were a few people, but the Calle de Antón Trillo was deserted. We stopped close to the wall of the garden and listened attentively. All was in such deep silence that the house seemed abandoned. Could it really be abandoned? Although this quarter was one of those least damaged by the bombardment, many families had left it, or were living as refugees in their own cellars.

===Chinese===
我们哄过了那老头子，便动身走了。夜已经很深——我方才讲述的那场葬礼，足足持续了三个多小时。火光已经望不见了。新塔硕大的塔身没入沉沉夜色，那口大钟也不再作响，只是偶尔响上一声，通报一颗炸弹的来临。不多时我们就到了圣费利佩小广场。望见教堂旁一座房子的屋顶还在冒烟，我们便明白了：三个钟头前遭火舌吞噬的是这一座，而不是坎迪奥拉的宅子。

“上帝保住了它！”奥古斯丁欣然喊道，“倘若她父亲的刻薄会招来天怒、降罪那片屋顶，玛丽基亚的美德与无辜也会保住它！我们到那儿去吧。”

圣费利佩小广场上还有寥寥几个人，安东·特里约街却空无一人。我们贴着花园的墙根站住，凝神细听。四下里一片死寂，整座宅子像是人去屋空。当真空了吗？这一区虽属炮击受损最轻的地方之一，许多人家还是迁走了，另一些则躲进自家地窖，过着难民的日子。

===Original===
“If I go in,” said Augustine to me, “you must come in with me. After the scene of today, I am afraid that Don Jerónimo, suspicious and cowardly, like a good miser, will be up all night and about his garden, lest they return and carry off his whole place.”

“In that case it is better not to go in,” I answered, “because besides the danger of falling into the hands of that old scoundrel, there would be a great scandal, and all Saragossa will know that the son of Don José Montoria, the young man destined for a bishop’s mitre, goes by night to see the daughter of the goodman Candiola.”

But this and all that I could say to him was like preaching in the desert. Without listening to reason, and insisting that I should follow him, he made the signal of love, waiting and watching with great anxiety for the reply. Some time passed, and at last, after long looking and looking again from the pavement in front, we saw a light in a high window. We heard the fastening of the gate drawn back softly, and it was opened without creaking. Love had taken precautions, and the ancient hinges had been oiled. We two entered meeting, unexpectedly, not a perfumed and fascinating damsel, but a vinegary countenance which I recognized at once as that of Doña Guedita.

===Chinese===
“我要是进去，”奥古斯丁对我说，“你也得陪我一起进去。经过白天那一场，我怕唐·赫罗尼莫疑心重、胆子小——十足一个地道的守财奴——今夜准会整宿不睡，在园子里巡查个不停，唯恐他们去而复来，把他的家当劫掠一空。”

“那样的话，还是不进去的好，”我答道，“因为除了有落进那老恶棍手里的危险，还会闹出一场大大的风波，全萨拉戈萨都会知道：唐·何塞·蒙特里亚的儿子、那位眼看要戴上主教冠的年轻人，深更半夜去会老好人坎迪奥拉的女儿。”

可是我这话，连同我所能说的一切，都无异于向旷野布道。他听不进道理，非要我跟去不可，一面打出了那爱情的暗号，焦灼地守望着回音。过了一阵，我们终于——在宅子前面的人行道上一望再望之后——望见高处一扇窗里亮起了灯。我们听见门闩被轻轻抽开，门开时没有一丝吱呀声。爱情早有防备：那古老的合页，都上过油了。我们两人走进去，迎面而来的，出乎意料，不是什么熏香扑鼻、勾人心魄的小姐，却是一张酸溜溜的面孔——我一眼认出，那正是堂娜·格韦迪塔。

===Original===
“He lets hours pass before he comes, and then he comes with another,” she grumbled. “Young men, be so kind as to make no noise. Walk on tiptoe, and be careful not to stumble over even a dried leaf, because Señor Candiola seems to me to be very wide awake.”

This she said to us in a voice so low that we heard with difficulty; then she went on before, making signs that we should follow her, putting her finger to her lips to enjoin absolute silence. The garden was small. We soon crossed it, and came to the stone staircase which led up to the doorway of the house. Here there came to meet us a shapely figure wrapped in a mantle, or cloak. It was Mariquilla! Her first gesture was to impose silence, indicating with anxiety, as I saw, a window which opened upon the garden. She then showed surprise that Augustine had not come unaccompanied. But he knew how to soothe her, saying, “It is Gabriel, my best, my only friend, of whom I have spoken so many times.”

“Speak lower,” whispered Mariquilla; “my father went out of his room a little while ago with a lantern, and made the rounds of the house and the garden. I doubt if he is asleep yet. The night is dark. Let us hide in the shadow of the cypress, and talk in a very low voice.”

===Chinese===
“他让人等了几个钟头才肯来，来了还带着个伴儿，”她嘟囔道，“两位少爷，行行好，千万别弄出声响。踮着脚尖走，留神别绊着哪怕一片干树叶——依我看，坎迪奥拉先生醒得很呢。”

这话她声音压得极低，我们竖起耳朵才勉强听清；随后她在前头引路，打手势叫我们跟上，一根手指抵着嘴唇，示意绝对噤声。园子不大，我们很快穿过去，来到通往宅门的石阶前。迎面走来一个裹着披风的窈窕身影——是玛丽基亚！她的第一个手势便是叫人噤声，神情焦灼地指了指一扇朝园子开的窗——我瞧得分明。接着，见奥古斯丁不是独自前来，她面露讶异。但他自有安抚的法子，说道：“这是加夫列尔，我最要好、唯一的朋友，我跟你提起过多少回的。”

“说话再小声点，”玛丽基亚悄声道，“方才我父亲提着灯笼出了房门，把屋子和园子都巡查了一遍。我看他这会儿还没睡着。夜这么黑。我们躲到柏树的影子里去，压低了嗓子说话吧。”

===Original===
The stone stairway led up to a kind of balcony with a wooden railing. The great cypress in the garden cast a deep shadow at the end of the balcony, forming there a refuge against the clear light of the moon. The bare boughs of an elm spread above the other end of the balcony, casting a thousand fantastic shadows upon the floor, upon the walls of the house, and upon ourselves. In the protection of the cypress, Mariquilla seated herself upon the only seat that was there, and Montoria threw himself upon the floor beside her, resting his hands upon her knees. I seated myself also upon the floor not far from the pair. It was a January night, still, dry, and cold. Perhaps the two lovers with hearts aflame did not feel the low temperature; but I, a creature stranger to their fires, wrapped myself in my cloak to keep myself from the chill of the tiles. Guedita had disappeared. Mariquilla led the conversation, plunging at once into the difficulty.

“I saw you in the street this morning. When Guedita and I heard the noise of the people crowding about our gate, I went to the window, and I saw you on the sidewalk in front.”

“It is true, I was there,” replied Montoria, with emotion, “but I was obliged to go at once. I couldn’t stand it.”

“Didn’t you see how those barbarians were trampling my father underfoot? When that cruel man struck him, I looked everywhere, hoping that you would come forward in his defence. But I did not see you anywhere.”

===Chinese===
石阶通到一处带木栏杆的平台上，算是个阳台。园中那株高大的柏树在阳台一端投下浓影，正好遮住皎洁的月光，成了一处藏身之地。一株榆树光秃秃的枝桠在阳台另一端上方铺展开来，把千姿百态的奇影投在地面上、宅墙上，也投在我们身上。玛丽基亚在柏树的荫庇下，在那儿仅有的一张座位上坐了；蒙特里亚挨着她往地上一坐，双手搭在她的膝头。我也在离这对情侣不远的地方席地而坐。那是一月的夜晚，无风，干燥而寒冷。这一对心火熊熊的恋人，或许并不觉得寒意逼人；我呢，与他们那团火焰无缘的人，只好裹紧斗篷，抵挡地砖透上来的寒气。格韦迪塔不知去向。玛丽基亚先开了口，一开口便直入那桩为难的事。

“今天早上我在街上看见你了。格韦迪塔和我听见一群人拥在我们门口喧嚷，我便走到窗前，看见你就在门前的人行道上。”

“不错，我是在那儿，”蒙特里亚动情地答道，“可我不得不立刻走开。我受不了。”

“你没看见那些野蛮人怎样把我父亲踩在脚下吗？那个狠心的家伙动手打他的时候，我四处张望，盼着你能挺身出来护他。可我哪儿也望不见你。”

===Original===
“I tell you, Mariquilla of my heart,” said Augustine, “that I was obliged to go. After they told me that your father had been so ill-treated, I came as soon as I could get a chance.”

“A pretty time to come! Among so many, so many people,” said Mariquilla, weeping, “not one lifted a hand to help him. I nearly died of fright, seeing him in such danger. I looked anxiously into the street, and there was no one but enemies, no one; not one kind hand or voice among all those men! One of them, more cruel than all, knocked my father down. Oh, oh, remembering this, I scarcely know what happened next! When I saw it, my fright paralyzed me for a few moments. Until then I never knew what violent anger was, how a sudden impulse, an inward fire, could drive me on. I came to him. My poor father was lying on the ground, and the wretch was trampling upon him as if he were a venomous reptile. When I saw that, I felt my blood boil in my veins. As I have told you, I ran about the house, looking for a weapon, a knife, an axe, anything. When I heard the cries of my father, I flew down. Finding myself among so many men, I felt a strange, uncontrollable timidity, and could not stir a step. The same man who had kicked him handed me a fistful of gold. I did not want to take it, but I did; then I threw all the coins into his face, with all my strength. My hand was as if filled with thunderbolts, and I felt as if I were avenging my father, hurling them at that villain. I went out afterwards, looking everywhere for you; but I could not see you. I found my father alone in that inhuman crowd, down in the mud, begging for mercy.”

“Oh, Mariquilla, Mariquilla of my heart!” cried Augustine in anguish, kissing the hands of the unhappy daughter of the miser. “Don’t talk any more about all that. You tear my soul in two! I could not defend him. I﻿—I had to go. I believed the crowd was after something﻿—else. You are right. But let us talk no more of this which grieves me so, and gives me such bitter pain.”

===Chinese===
“我告诉你，我心里的玛丽基亚，”奥古斯丁说，“我实在是不得不走。后来听说你父亲受了那样的虐待，我一得着空子，就尽快赶来了。”

“这时候赶来，可真是会挑时候！那么多人，那么多的人啊，”玛丽基亚哭着说，“竟没有一个肯抬一抬手帮帮他。眼看他陷在那样危险的境地，我吓得魂儿都要没了。我焦急地朝街上张望，满眼净是仇人，净是仇人；那么些男人当中，没有一只善良的手，没有一个善良的声音！其中一个，比谁都狠毒，把我父亲打倒在地。唉，唉，如今想起这一幕，后来发生了什么，我简直记不真切了！亲眼看见的时候，我吓得有一阵子动弹不得。在那以前，我从不知道什么叫狂怒，不知道一股突来的冲动、一团心底的火，能把我驱使得那样凶。我下楼到了他那儿。我可怜的父亲倒在地上，那恶棍正踩在他身上，仿佛踩一条毒虫。一看见那光景，我只觉得浑身的血都在血管里沸腾起来。像我方才告诉过你的，我在屋里四处乱转，找一件家伙，一把刀，一柄斧头，什么都行。听见父亲的呼号，我飞奔下楼。可一到那么多男人中间，我竟生出一种说不出的、按捺不住的怯意，一步也挪不动了。正是踢倒他的那个男人，捧来一把金币递给我。我本不想接，却接了；接着我便抡圆了力气，把那些金币统统摔在他脸上。我那只手仿佛攥满了雷霆，只觉得自己是在替父亲报仇，把它们一股脑儿掷向那个恶棍。随后我出了门，四处找你；可怎么也找不见你。到头来，我只找到我父亲孤零零一个人陷在那群没有人性的暴徒中间，倒在泥水里，讨饶求告。”

“唉，玛丽基亚，我心里的玛丽基亚！”奥古斯丁痛苦地喊道，一面亲吻着这吝啬鬼的不幸的女儿的双手。“别再讲这些了。你把我的魂都撕成两半了！我没法护他。我——我不得不走。我当时以为，那群人围的是——别的事情。你说得对。可是咱们别再提这桩事了，它叫我这么伤心，这么锥心地痛。”

===Original===
“If you had come to the defence of my father, he would have felt gratitude towards you. From gratitude one passes readily to affection. You would have been received openly in the house.”

“Your father is incapable of affection for anyone,” replied Montoria. “Do not hope that we can accomplish anything in that way. Let us trust that we may arrive at the fulfilment of our desires by hidden ways, perhaps by the help of God when it least seems likely. Let us not depend upon aught else, or think of what is before us. We are surrounded by dangers and obstacles that seem unsurmountable. Let us hope for help from the unseen, and filled by faith in God and the power of our love, let us wait for the miracle which will unite us. For it will be a miracle, Mariquilla, a wonder like those they tell of in olden times, that we refuse to believe.”

“A miracle!” exclaimed Mariquilla, sadly. “It is true. You are a young gentleman of position, the son of parents who would never consent to see you married to the daughter of Señor Candiola. My father is abhorred all over the city. Everybody flees from us. No one visits us. If I go out they point at me, and look at me with insolent contempt. Girls of my own age will not associate with me, and the young men of the city who go about singing serenades under the windows of their sweethearts, come to mine to utter insults against my father, calling me also dreadful names to my face. Oh, my God, I understand that it would be indeed a miracle for me to be happy! Augustine, we have known each other now for nearly four months, and you have not yet told me the name of your parents. It certainly cannot be as odious as mine. Why do you hide it? If it were necessary that our love should be made public, you would not dare meet the looks of your friends, you would flee with horror from the daughter of Candiola.”

===Chinese===
“那天你要是出来护了我父亲，他对你必会心生感激。由感激到亲近，原是轻易就能跨过去的一步。你也就能堂堂正正地进这道门了。”

“你父亲那个人，对谁也生不出亲情，”蒙特里亚答道，“别指望走这条路能成事。我们且信着，凭一条条幽隐的小径，终能如愿以偿——或许靠上帝之助，就在最不像会有转机的时候。别的什么都不要倚仗，眼前这些也不必去多想。我们四周围着的，尽是看似无法逾越的险阻与障碍。那就指望冥冥之中的扶助吧；怀着对上帝和我们爱情之伟力的信心，静候那个将我们结为一体的奇迹。因为那必得是一个奇迹，玛丽基亚，就像古时候人们讲起、而我们自己都不肯信的那些奇事一样。”

“奇迹！”玛丽基亚悲戚地叹道，“这话不假。你是位有身份的少爷，是那样一对父母的儿子——他们断断不能容你娶坎迪奥拉先生的女儿。我父亲在全城人人切齿。谁都躲着我们。没有一个人登我家的门。我一出门，人家就对我指指点点，拿放肆轻蔑的眼光看我。同龄的姑娘们不肯与我往来；城里的年轻人，在各自情人的窗下唱着小夜曲，到了我的窗下，却是来辱骂我父亲的，还当面给我起种种难听的名字。唉，我的上帝，我明白，我要想得到幸福，那真得有一个奇迹！奥古斯丁，我们相识如今快四个月了，你还没有告诉我你父母的名姓。他们总不会像我父亲这般招人恨吧。你为什么瞒着？倘若我们的爱情有朝一日不得不公之于众，你定然不敢正视你那些朋友的目光，你会心怀惊怖，从坎迪奥拉的女儿身边逃开。”

===Original===
“Oh, no, don’t say that!” cried Augustine, pressing against Mariquilla, and hiding his face in her lap. “Don’t say that I am ashamed of loving you. In saying that you insult God. It is not true. Today our love remains a secret, because it is necessary that it should be so. But when it is necessary to make it known, I will make it known, and defy the anger of my father face to face. Yes, Mariquilla, my parents will curse me, and turn me out of doors. A few nights ago you said to me, looking at that monument which we can see from here, ‘When that tower becomes straight, I will leave off loving you.’ I swear to you that the strength of my love is more immovable than the equilibrium of yonder tower; for that could fall to the ground, but could never stand upright. The works of man are variable, those of Nature are unchangeable and rest evermore upon an everlasting base. You have seen the Moncayo, that great rock which is near Poniente in the suburb? Well, when Moncayo gets tired of being in that place, and moves and comes walking towards Saragossa, putting one of its feet upon our city and reducing it to powder, then and then only will I cease to love you.”

By this sort of hyperbole and poetic naturalism my friend expressed his great love, flattering the imagination of the beautiful girl, who responded, leaning forward, moved by an impulse like his own. They were both silent for a moment, then the two, or rather the three of us, exclaimed all together, looking at the tower whose belfry had flung to the winds two signals of alarm. At the same moment a globe of fire ploughed the black space, describing rapid circles.

“A bomb! It is a bomb,” exclaimed Mariquilla, trembling, and throwing herself into the arms of her lover. The dreadful light passed swiftly over our heads, over the garden and the house, illuminating on its way the tower, the neighboring houses, and the nook where we were hidden. Then the report was heard. The bell began to ring violently, and was joined by others near and far, loud, heavy, sharp, jangled; and we heard the noise of feet and voices of people in the nearest streets.

===Chinese===
“噢，别，别这么说！”奥古斯丁喊道，把身子凑近玛丽基亚，把脸埋进她的裙兜。“别说我以爱上你为耻。你这样说，是亵渎上帝。不是这样的。今天我们的爱情还是一桩秘密，因为眼下必须如此。可一旦需要把它公开，我就公开，并且直面我父亲的雷霆之怒。是的，玛丽基亚，我父母会咒骂我，会把我扫地出门。几天前的一个夜里，你望着我们从这里望得见的那座建筑，对我说：‘那座塔几时立直了，我便几时不爱你。’我向你发誓，我爱情的坚贞，比那座塔的平衡更加不可移易；因为那塔还有倒地的一天，却断无立直的可能。人工之物善变，造化之物不移，永远安坐在亘古的基座上。你见过蒙卡约吧，就是城郊那面、西天（Poniente）方向的那座巨岩？好——等蒙卡约在那地方待腻了，挪动身子，一步一步朝萨拉戈萨走来，一只脚踏上我们的城，把它踩成齑粉，到那时，也只有到那时，我才会不爱你。”

我这位朋友，便是用这一类夸张而富于诗意的自然之喻，倾吐他的深情，去撩动那美丽姑娘的想象；她也报以同样的心潮，身子前倾，凑近了他。两人静默了片刻，随后我们两个——不如说我们三个——不约而同齐声惊呼，一齐望向那座塔：塔顶的钟楼方才向夜空抛出了两声警报。就在同一刻，一团火球犁开黑暗的夜空，划出一圈圈急转的弧光。

“炸弹！是颗炸弹！”玛丽基亚颤声叫道，扑进情人的怀抱。那可怕的光从我们头顶急掠而过，掠过园子和宅子，一路照亮了高塔、左近的房舍，也照亮了我们藏身的那个角落。接着，爆响传来。大钟开始狂敲，远近的钟声纷纷加入，洪亮的、沉浊的、尖利的，乱作一片；我们还听见近处几条街里，响起奔跑的脚步声和嘈杂的人声。

===Original===
“That bomb will not kill us,” said Augustine, soothing his sweetheart. “Are you afraid?”

“Yes, very, very much afraid,” she answered. “I spend the nights praying, asking God to keep the fire away from our house. Until now no misfortune has come near us, either now or in the other siege. But how many unhappy ones have perished, how many houses of good people who never harmed anyone have been destroyed by the flames! I long earnestly to go like other women and take care of the suffering; but my father forbids me, and is angry with me whenever I propose it.”

As she said this, we heard within the house a distant sound of talking, in which the harsh tones of Candiola were mingled with the voice of Guedita. We three, obeying one impulse, drew into the shadow and held our breaths, fearing to be surprised. Then we heard the voice of the miser coming nearer, and saying﻿—

===Chinese===
“那颗炸弹伤不着我们，”奥古斯丁宽慰他的情人道，“你害怕吗？”

“怕，怕得很，怕得很，”她答道，“我夜夜祈祷，求上帝别让火落进我们家。直到如今，无论这一次围城还是上一次，灾殃都没有挨近过我们。可是多少不幸的人丧了命，多少从不害人的好人家的宅子，都毁在烈火里了！我真心想像别的女人那样，去照护那些受苦受难的人；可我父亲不许，我一提这事他就动气。”

她正这样说着，我们听见宅子深处传来隐隐的说话声，坎迪奥拉那粗厉的嗓门，混在格韦迪塔的声音里。我们三个凭着同一股本能，缩进暗影里，屏住呼吸，生怕被人撞见。接着，便听见那吝啬鬼的声音近了，说道——

===Original===
“What are you doing up at this hour, Señora Guedita?”

“Señor,” answered the old woman, showing herself at a window which opened upon the balcony, “who can sleep during this dreadful bombardment? Perhaps a bomb may come and meddle with us here. What if the house should take fire, and the neighbors should come to drag out the furniture and put out the fire, and find us in our nightclothes? Oh, what a lack of modesty! I do not intend to undress myself while this devilish bombardment lasts.”

“Is my daughter asleep?” asked Candiola, appearing at another garden window.

“She is upstairs sleeping like a kitten,” replied the duenna. “They speak truly when they say that there are no dangers for innocence. A bomb does not frighten the child any more than a skyrocket.”

===Chinese===
“这么晚了你不睡，在做什么呢，格韦迪塔太太？”

“先生，”老妇人答道，一面在朝阳台开的一扇窗前露出身影，“这样可怕的炮击，谁睡得着？说不定就会有一颗炸弹飞来，搅到我们头上。万一宅子着了火，邻居们闯进来拖家具、救火，撞见我们穿着睡衣，那成什么体统？唉，多不害臊！这可恶的炮击一天不结束，我就一天不打算脱衣裳。”

“我女儿睡了吗？”坎迪奥拉在朝园子开的另一扇窗前露面，问道。

“她在楼上，睡得像只小猫，”老嬷嬷答道，“人说无辜的人百无禁忌，这话不假。炸弹吓不着这孩子，就跟一支烟花吓不着她一样。”

===Original===
“I wonder if I can see from here where the projectile has fallen,” said Candiola, stretching his body out of the window, in order to be able to extend his range of vision. “I can see the light of a fire, but I cannot say whether it is near or far.”

“Oh, I don’t know anything about bombs,” said Guedita, who had come out on the balcony. “This one has fallen over there by the market.”

“So it seems. If only all would fall upon the houses of those who persist in keeping up the defence and causing the destruction! If I am not deceived, Señora Guedita, the fire is near the Calle de la Tripería. Are not the storehouses of the junta of supplies over there? Oh, blessed bomb, why not fall into the Calle de la Hilarza, upon the house of that cursed, most miserable thief! Señora Guedita, I am going to the Calle de la Hilarza, to see if it has fallen on the house of that proud, meddlesome, cowardly thief, Don José de Montoria. I have prayed for it tonight to the Virgin del Pilar with so much fervor, and also at the Santas Masas and at Santo Dominguito del Val, that at last I believe I have been heard.”

“Señor Don Jerónimo,” said the old woman, “do not go out! The cold of the night is bad for you, and it is not worth risking your lungs to see where the bomb has fallen. It is enough that it has not meddled with this house. If that one which passed did not fall into the house of that barbarian of an official, another will fall tomorrow. The French have a good handful. Now, your honor, go to rest. I will stay up and look after the house.”

===Chinese===
“不知从这儿望不望得见那颗炮弹落在哪儿，”坎迪奥拉说着，把身子探出窗外，好让眼界放远些，“我望得见一处火光，可说不上是远是近。”

“唉，炮弹的事我一窍不通，”格韦迪塔说，这时她已出到阳台上，“这一颗落在那边，市场左近。”

“看样子是。但愿炮弹颗颗都落在那些死抱着防御不放、招来这场浩劫的人家房顶上！要是我没认错，格韦迪塔太太，火光就在特里佩里亚街（Calle de la Tripería）左近。补给议会的仓库，不就在那一带吗？噢，蒙福的炸弹，你怎么不落进伊拉尔萨街，落进那个可诅咒的、顶顶卑劣的窃贼家里！格韦迪塔太太，我要到伊拉尔萨街去，看看炮弹是不是落在了那个骄横、多事、怯懦的窃贼唐·何塞·德·蒙特里亚的房上。今夜我为这事向皮拉尔圣母祈求，求得多恳切啊——还在桑塔斯·马萨斯（Santas Masas）和圣多明尼基托·德尔·瓦尔（Santo Dominguito del Val）两处也求了——到如今我相信，总算蒙垂听了。”

“唐·赫罗尼莫先生，”老妇人说，“千万别出门！夜里的寒气对您有害，为着看一颗炸弹落在哪儿，犯不上拿您的肺去冒险。它没来搅扰这宅子，就该知足了。方才过去的那一颗，若是没落进那个当官的蛮子家里，明天还会再落一颗。法国佬手里有一大把呢。好了，老爷，回屋歇着去吧。我留下来守夜，照看宅子。”

===Original===
Candiola changed his mind about going out, it seemed, in accordance with the good counsels of his servant, and, shutting the window, he was heard no more during all the rest of the night. But although he disappeared, the lovers did not break the silence, fearful of being overheard. And not until the old woman came to tell us that the señor was snoring like a peasant was the interrupted dialogue continued.

“My father wished that the bombs would fall upon the house of his enemy,” said Mariquilla. “I should not like to see them fall anywhere; but if at any time one could wish ill-fortune to a neighbor, it would be now, do you not think so?”

Augustine made no answer.

===Chinese===
坎迪奥拉像是听从了老仆的良言，打消了出门的念头；关上窗以后，这一夜余下的时辰再没听见他的声息。他人虽隐去了，这对恋人却依旧不敢出声，唯恐被人听了去。直到老妇人前来告诉我们，说老爷已经鼾声如雷，睡得像个庄稼汉，中断了的情话才续了下去。

“我父亲巴不得炸弹落在他仇人的宅子上，”玛丽基亚说，“我倒不愿意看见炸弹落在任何地方；不过，人若真有可以咒邻人遭殃的时候，如今倒正是时候，你说是不是？”

奥古斯丁没有作声。

===Original===
“You went away. You did not see how that man, the most cruel, the most cowardly of all who came, knocked him down in his blind fury, and trampled upon him. The fiends will kick his soul in hell like that, won’t they?”

“Yes,” replied the young man, laconically.

“Today, after it all, Guedita and I dressed the wounds of my father. He was stretched upon his bed, crazy, desperate. He was twisting about, gnawing his fists and lamenting that he was not stronger than his enemy. We tried to console him, but he told us to be silent. He struck me in the face, he was so angry when he heard that I had thrown away the money for the flour. He was furious with me. He told me that since he could not get any more, the three thousand reales on account should not be despised. He said that I am a spendthrift, and am ruining him. We could not calm him in any way. Towards nightfall we heard another noise in the street, and were afraid that the same ones who were here in the middle of the day were returning. My father was raging, and determined to get up. I was greatly frightened; but I took courage, realizing that courage was necessary. Thinking of you, I said, ‘If he were in the house, no one would dare insult us.’ As the noise in the street increased, I plucked up all my courage. I shut and fastened the doors and gates, and, begging my father to keep quiet in his bed, I waited, resolved. While Guedita was on her knees, praying to all the saints in heaven, I searched the house for a weapon. At last I found a big knife. The sight of it has always frightened me, but today I clutched it fearlessly. Oh, I was beside myself! Now the very thought of it makes me frightened. I am usually unable to look upon a wounded man, and tremble at the sight of a drop of blood. I almost cry if I see anyone beat a dog before me. I have never had the force to kill a mosquito. But this evening, Augustine, this evening, when I heard the noise in the street, when I thought those blows upon the gate had come again, when I expected every moment to see those men before me, I swear to you that if that had happened which I feared, if when I was in my father’s room, by his bedside, if that same man had come who abused him a few hours before, I swear to you that there I myself would have struck him through the heart.”

“Hush, for God’s sake!” cried Montoria, horrified. “You frighten me. Hearing you, you almost make me feel as if your own hands, these divine hands, struck cold steel through my breast. Nobody will maltreat your father again. You see already that your alarm of tonight was nothing but fright. No, you would not have been capable of what you say.
