I looked at the tower, and, naturally, the tower was still leaning.

“Gabriel,” said Augustine, “kill me! I do not want to live. No, I will not take life from that man. You must take the order. I, if I live, must run away. I am sick. I will tear off these epaulets, and throw them in the face of General Saint March. No, do not tell me that the Torre Nueva is still leaning. Why, man, do you not see that it is straight? My friend, you deceive me. My heart is pierced as by red-hot steel, and my blood burns within me. I am dying of the pain.”

===Chinese===

奥古斯丁没有听见我的话。他像个疯子一般，从我身边挣脱开去，一转眼又折了回来，嘴里吐着绝望的话，然后望向那座塔——它壮丽而高峻，在我们头顶上拔地耸立——他满眼惊恐地喊道——

“加夫列尔，你难道看不见吗？你没看见那座塔吗？你没看见它是直的吗，加夫列尔？塔已经扶直了！”

我望了望那座塔；自然，塔依旧倾斜着。

“加夫列尔，”奥古斯丁说，“杀了我吧！我不想活了。不，我不去取那个人的性命。你必须把那道命令接过去。我呢，只要还活着，就得逃走。我病了。我要扯下这对肩章，摔到圣马尔奇将军的脸上去。不，别再对我说新塔还斜着。怎么，老兄，你难道看不见它是直的吗？我的朋友，你在骗我。我的心像被烧红的钢铁穿透了，血在体内烧灼。我快要痛死了。”
===Original===

I was trying to console him, when a white figure entered the plaza by the Calle de Torresecas. On seeing her I trembled, for it was Mariquilla. Augustine did not have time to flee, and the distressed girl embraced him, exclaiming eagerly in her emotion﻿—

“Augustine! Augustine! thank God, I have found you here! How much I love you! When they told me that you were the jailer of my father, I was wild with delight, for I know that you will save him. Those savages of the council have condemned him to death. He to die who has done harm to no one! But God does not wish the innocent to perish, and He has put him in your hands, so that you may let him escape!”

“Oh, my heart’s Mariquilla,” said Augustine, “leave me, I pray you! I don’t wish to see you. Tomorrow﻿—tomorrow we will talk. I love you, too. I am mad for you. Let Saragossa perish, but don’t leave off loving me! They expected me to kill your father.”

“Oh, God, do not say that!” cried the girl. “Thou!”

“No, a thousand times no! Let others punish his treachery.”

“No, it is a lie! My father is not a traitor. Do you also accuse him? I never have believed it. Augustine, it is night. Untie his hands; take off the fetters that hurt his feet. Set him at liberty. No one can see. We will flee. We will hide ourselves in the ruins of our house, there by the cypress where so many times we have seen the spire of the Torre Nueva.”

===Chinese===

我正想安慰他，忽见一个白色的身影从托雷塞卡斯街（Calle de Torresecas）走进广场。一看见她我就浑身一颤——是玛丽基亚。奥古斯丁来不及躲开，那悲痛欲绝的姑娘一把抱住了他，激动而急切地喊道——

“奥古斯丁！奥古斯丁！感谢上帝，可叫我在这儿找到你了！我是多么爱你啊！他们告诉我，看押我父亲的人就是你，我欢喜得发了狂，因为我知道你会救他的。军事法庭那帮野蛮人判了他死刑。他这个从没害过一个人的人，竟要去死！可是上帝不愿让无辜者丧命，他把父亲交在了你手里，就是要你放他逃走！”

“唉，我心中的玛丽基亚，”奥古斯丁说，“我求你离开我吧！我不想见你。明天——明天我们再谈。我也爱你。我爱你爱得发疯。让萨拉戈萨毁灭去吧，只求你别停止爱我！他们竟指望我杀死你的父亲。”

“噢，上帝，别说这话！”姑娘喊道，“你！”

“不，一千个不！让旁人去惩罚他的叛逆吧。”

“不，那是谎话！我父亲不是叛徒。连你也指控他吗？我从来不信这一套。奥古斯丁，现在是夜里。给他解开双手吧；给他卸下磨脚的镣铐吧。放他自由。没有人看得见。我们一起逃。我们藏到我们家宅的废墟里去，就在那棵柏树旁边——多少回，我们在那里望得见新塔的塔尖。”

===Original===

“Mariquilla, wait a little,” said Montoria, with great agitation. “This cannot be done so. There are many people in the plaza. The soldiers are greatly incensed against the prisoner. Tomorrow﻿—”

“Tomorrow! What do you say? You are laughing at me. Set him at liberty this instant. Augustine, if you do not do it, I shall believe that I have loved the most vile, the most cowardly, the most despicable of men.”

“Mariquilla, God hears us. God knows that I adore you. By Him I swear that I will not stain my hands with the blood of this unhappy man. I will sooner break my sword. But﻿—in the name of God, I tell you also that I cannot set your father at liberty. Mariquilla, Heaven is against us.”

“Augustine, you are deceiving me,” said the girl, anguished and bewildered. “Do you tell me that you will not set him at liberty?”

“Oh, no, I cannot. If God should come in human form to ask of me the freedom of him who sold our heroic peasants, delivered them up to the French sword, I would not do it. It is a supreme duty, in which one cannot fail. The innumerable victims immolated by his treachery, the city surrendered, the national honor outraged, are things which weigh too strongly upon my conscience.”

===Chinese===

“玛丽基亚，稍等一等，”蒙特里亚极为激动地说，“这事不能这么办。广场上人多得很。士兵们对这囚犯恨之入骨。明天——”

“明天！你说什么？你在耍笑我。立刻就放他自由。奥古斯丁，你要是不这么做，我就只好相信，我爱上的是天下最早鄙、最怯懦、最下作的男人。”

“玛丽基亚，上帝听着我们呢。上帝知道我多么爱你。我凭着他起誓，决不让我这双手沾上这不幸之人的血。我宁可折断我的剑。可是——我也凭着上帝的名义告诉你，我不能放你的父亲自由。玛丽基亚，上天在同我们作对。”

“奥古斯丁，你在骗我，”姑娘说，痛苦而惶惑，“你是要告诉我，你不肯放他自由？”

“噢，不，我办不到。哪怕上帝化成人身，亲自来向我讨这个人的自由——他把我们英勇的乡民出卖，送到法国人的刀剑之下——我也不答应。这是至高无上的天职，谁也不能失守。他的叛逆戕害的无数牺牲者，沦陷的城池，蒙辱的国誉，这一切压在我的良心上，重得叫我担不起。”

===Original===

“My father cannot have done this deed of treachery,” she said, passing at once from grief to an exalted and nervous anger; “these are calumnies of his enemies. They lie who call him traitor, and you, more cruel and more inhuman than all, you lie also! It is not possible that I have loved you! It causes me shame to think of it. You say you will not free him? Then of what good are you? Do you hope to gain favor by your bloody cruelty of those inhuman barbarians who have destroyed the city, imagining that they were defending it? To you the life of the innocent is of no consequence, nor the desolation of an orphan. Miserable, ambitious egoist, I abhor you more than I have ever loved you! You thought that you would be able to present yourself before me with your hands stained with the blood of my father? No, he is not a traitor. You are traitors, all of you. My God, is there no generous hand to help me? Among so many men, is there not even one to prevent this crime? A poor woman runs through all the city looking for a friendly soul, and does not find anything but wild beasts.”

“Mariquilla,” said Augustine, “you are lacerating my soul. You ask the impossible of me, that which I will not do, and cannot do, although you offer me eternal blessedness for payment. I have sacrificed all, and I knew that you would abhor me. Think what it is for a man to tear out his own heart and trample it in the mud. I have done that. I can do no more.”

The fervent exaltation of Mariquilla Candiola carried her from intense anger to pathetic sensitiveness of suffering. She had showed her anger with fiery heat, now she burst into bitter tears, expressing herself thus﻿—

===Chinese===

“我父亲决不可能干出这等叛逆的勾当，”她说，霎时间从悲痛转成激愤的狂怒；“这些都是他仇家的诬陷。骂他叛徒的人都在撒谎，而你，比他们全都更残忍、更没有人性，你也在撒谎！我竟会爱过你，这不可能！想一想都叫我羞愧。你说你不肯放他？那你还有什么用处？你想靠这血腥的残忍，去讨好那帮灭了这座城、还只道自己是在守城的没有人性的野蛮畜生吗？无辜者的性命，孤女的凄凉，在你眼里都无足轻重。卑鄙的、野心勃勃的自私鬼，我如今恨你，恨得远胜过我从前爱你！你以为，你还能双手沾着我父亲的血，走到我面前来吗？不，他不是叛徒。你们才是叛徒，你们全体。我的上帝，就没有一只慷慨的手来帮帮我吗？这么多男人里面，就连一个肯出来拦阻这桩罪行的都没有吗？一个可怜的女人跑遍全城寻觅一颗好心肠，寻见的却只有豺狼虎豹。”

“玛丽基亚，”奥古斯丁说，“你是在剐我的灵魂。你向我要的是办不到的事，是我不肯做、也不能做的事，哪怕你拿永恒的至福来偿我。我已经牺牲了一切，而且我早知道你会憎恶我。想一想吧，一个人亲手把自己的心掏出来、踩进泥里，那是什么滋味。我已经这样做了。我再也无能为力了。”

玛丽基亚·坎迪奥拉那炽烈的激情，把她从极度的狂怒带进了楚楚可怜的哀痛。她已用烈火般的言语发过了怒，此刻则涌出辛酸的眼泪，这样说道——

===Original===

“What mad things I have said! And what madness hast thou said, Augustine! How I have loved you, and how I do love you!﻿—from the time I saw you first at our house. You have never been absent from my thoughts for a moment. You have been to me the most loving, the most generous, the most thoughtful, the bravest of all men. I loved you without knowing who you were. I did not know your name, or that of your parents; but I would have loved you if you had been the son of the hangman of Saragossa. Augustine, you have forgotten me since we have not been together. It is I, Mariquilla. I have all this time believed, and I believe now that you will not take away from me my good father whom I love as much as I love you. He is good. He has not hurt anybody. He is a poor old man. He has some faults; but I do not see them. I do not see anything in him but virtues. I never knew my mother, who died when I was very small. I have lived retired from the world. My father has brought me up in solitude. In solitude the great love that I bear you has been nourished. If I had never known you, the whole world would have been nothing to me without him!”

I could read clearly Montoria’s indecision in his face. He was looking with terrified eyes, now at the girl, now at the sentinels at the entrance of the tower. The daughter of Candiola, with admirable instinct, knew how to make use of that evidence of weakness. Throwing her arms around his neck, she cried﻿—

“Augustine, set him at liberty! We will hide where no one can find us. If they say anything to you, if they accuse you of having failed in duty, do not take any notice of them. Come with me. How my father will love you, seeing you have saved his life! Then what happiness is before us, Augustine. How good you are! I was expecting it, and when I knew that the poor prisoner was in your hands, I felt the gates of heaven were open!”

My friend took a few steps, then drew back. There were plenty of soldiers and armed men in the plazuela. Suddenly there appeared before us a man on crutches, accompanied by several officials of high rank.

===Chinese===

“我方才说了些什么疯话啊！而你也说了些什么疯话，奥古斯丁！我爱过你多么深，如今又爱得多么深！——打我在我们家头一回见到你的时候起。你一刻也不曾离开过我的心头。在我眼里，你是所有男人里最深情、最慷慨、最体贴、最勇敢的一个。我爱上了你，却不知道你是谁。我不知道你的名字，也不知道你父母的名字；哪怕你是萨拉戈萨刽子手的儿子，我也会爱你的。奥古斯丁，自从我们不在一处，你就把我忘了。是我呀，玛丽基亚。我一直相信，如今也仍然相信，你不会夺走我的好父亲，我爱他同爱你一样深。他是善良的。他没有伤害过任何人。他是个可怜的老人。他有些毛病；可我看不见。我在他身上只看得见美德。我从没见过我母亲，我很小的时候她就死了。我一直离群索居地活着。父亲在孤寂中把我养大。我对你怀着的这份大爱，也是在孤寂中滋养起来的。倘若我从来不曾认识你，那么没有他，整个世界对我就什么都不是了！”

我清清楚楚看得出蒙特里亚脸上犹豫不决的神情。他用惊惶的眼睛，一会儿望望姑娘，一会儿望望塔门口的哨兵。坎迪奥拉的女儿凭着令人叹服的本能，懂得如何利用这软弱的一线痕迹。她伸出双臂搂住他的脖子，喊道——

“奥古斯丁，放他自由吧！我们藏到谁也找不着的地方去。若有人在你面前说三道四，说你玩忽职守，别去理会他们。跟我一起走。你救了他的命，我父亲会多么疼你啊！那时等着我们的，将是怎样的幸福啊，奥古斯丁。你多好啊！我本来就这般盼着，后来得知可怜的囚犯在你手里，我只觉得天堂的大门都打开了！”

我的朋友向前挪了几步，随即又缩了回来。小广场上有的是士兵和持械的人。忽然，一个拄着双拐的男人出现在我们面前，身后跟着几位高级军官。
===Original===

“What is going on here?” asked Don José de Montoria. “It seemed to me I heard the cries of a woman. Augustine, are you weeping? What is the matter?”

“Señor,” said Mariquilla, in alarm, turning to Montoria. “You will not at all oppose their setting my father at liberty? Do you not remember me? You were wounded yesterday, and I cared for you.”

“It is true, child,” said Don José gravely; “I am very grateful. Now I see that you are the daughter of Señor Candiola.”

“Yes, sir. Yesterday, when I was attending you, I recognized in you the man who ill-treated my father some time ago.”

“Yes, my daughter, it was a sudden thing﻿—a hasty﻿—I can’t help it. I have very quick blood. And you took care of me? That is the way good Christians do, returning good for evil, paying back injuries with benefits, and to do good to them that hate us is what God commands.”

“Señor,” exclaimed Mariquilla, dissolved in tears. “I forgive my enemies. Do you also forgive yours? Why do they not free my father? He has not done anything.”

===Chinese===

“这儿出了什么事？”唐·何塞·德·蒙特里亚问，“我好像听见一个女人的哭喊。奥古斯丁，你在哭吗？怎么回事？”

“先生，”玛丽基亚惊慌地转过身，对蒙特里亚说，“他们要放我父亲自由，您总不会反对吧？您不记得我了？您昨天受了伤，是我照料您的。”

“是真的，孩子，”唐·何塞庄重地说，“我很感激。现在我明白了，你是坎迪奥拉先生的女儿。”

“是的，先生。昨天我照料您的时候，就认出您来了——前些日子亏待我父亲的人，正是您。”

“是的，闺女，那是一时的事——一时火起——我自己也做不了主。我这血性来得急。这么说，是你照看了我？好基督徒就是这样做的：以德报怨，以恩还伤；向恨我们的人行善，正是上帝的吩咐。”

“先生，”玛丽基亚泪如雨下，喊道，“我宽恕我的仇人。您也宽恕您的仇人吧？他们为什么不放我父亲自由？他什么也没有做啊。”

===Original===

“This thing that you ask is a little difficult. The treachery of Señor Candiola is unpardonable. The troops are furious.”

“It is all a mistake. If you would intercede! You must be one of the commanders.”

“I!” said Montoria, “that is a business which does not rest on me. But calm yourself, young woman. You seem to be a good girl; truly, I remember the attention with which you took care of me, and such goodness touches my soul. I did you a great wrong, and from the same person whom I injured I received a great good, perhaps life itself. In such ways God teaches us to be humble and charitable, *porr*﻿—I was just going to let it go, this cursed tongue of mine!”

“Señor, how good you are!” cried the girl; “and I thought you were very bad. You will help me to save my father. He does not lay up the outrage he received.”

“Listen,” said Montoria, taking her by the arm. “Not long ago I asked pardon of Señor Don Jerónimo for all that; and far from being reconciled with me, he insulted me in the most gross manner. He and I do not pull together, child. If you tell me that you forgive me that matter of the blows, my conscience will be free of a great weight.”

===Chinese===

“你求的这件事，有点难办。坎迪奥拉先生的叛逆不可饶恕。士兵们都在气头上。”

“这全是一场误会。要是您肯说句话就好了！您想必是指挥官里头的人。”

“我！”蒙特里亚说，“那可不是归我管的事。不过你镇静些，姑娘。你看着是个好姑娘；说真的，我记着你照料我时那份尽心，这样的好心打动我的灵魂。我大大地亏负过你，而受了我亏负的人反倒给了我大恩，说不定连这条命都是她给的。上天就是这样教我们谦卑、教我们仁慈，*波*——瞧我这条该死的舌头，差点又让它溜了出去！”

“先生，您多好啊！”姑娘喊道，“我还当您非常坏呢。您会帮我救我父亲的。他并不记恨他受的那场欺辱。”

“听着，”蒙特里亚挽住她的胳膊说，“不多久以前，我为那件事向唐·赫罗尼莫先生赔过罪；他非但不肯与我和解，反倒用最粗鄙的方式辱骂了我一场。他和我合不来，孩子。如果你对我说一句，你饶恕了我动手打人那件事，我的良心便可卸下一副重担了。”

===Original===

“Indeed, there is nothing for me to forgive you. Oh, señor, how good you are! You command here surely. Then cause my father to be set free!”

“That is none of my business. Señor Candiola has committed a terrible crime. It is impossible to pardon him, impossible! I understand your affliction, and truly I feel it, especially in remembering your kindness. I will protect you. We shall see.”

“I do not wish for anything for myself,” said Mariquilla, whose voice was now hoarse with her emotion. “I only wish that an unfortunate man who has done nothing should be set at liberty. Augustine, are you not in command here? What are you doing?”

“This young man will do his duty,” said Montoria.

“This young man,” cried Mariquilla, angrily, “will do what I bid him, because he loves me. Isn’t it true that you will free my father? You said you would. Señors, what are you here for? Do you intend to stop him? Augustine, do not pay any attention to them; defend us!”

===Chinese===

“真的，我并没有什么可饶恕您的。噢，先生，您多好啊！这儿想必是您主事的。那就做主放我父亲自由吧！”

“那不归我管。坎迪奥拉先生犯下的是可怕的罪。赦免他是不可能的，不可能！你的苦楚我明白，说真的，我也感同身受，尤其一想起你的好心。我会保护你的。走着瞧吧。”

“我自己什么都不要，”玛丽基亚说，她的嗓音这时已因激动而嘶哑，“我只求放一个什么也没做过的可怜人自由。奥古斯丁，这儿不是你管着吗？你在做什么呀？”

“这位年轻人会尽职的，”蒙特里亚说。

“这位年轻人，”玛丽基亚怒冲冲地喊道，“会照我吩咐的去做，因为他爱我。你肯放我父亲的，是不是？你说过你肯的。先生们，你们站在这儿干什么？你们想拦住他吗？奥古斯丁，别理他们；保护我们！”

===Original===

“What is all this?” exclaimed Montoria, in amazement. “Augustine, have you told this girl that you have any idea of failing in your duty? Do you know her?”

Augustine, overcome by his fear, answered nothing.

“Yes, he will set him at liberty,” said Mariquilla, in despair. “Go away from here, señors. You have no business here.”

“What am I to understand?” cried Don José, seizing his son by his arm. “If what this girl says should be true, if I could imagine that my son’s honor could fail in this fashion, his loyalty sworn to his flag be trampled underfoot﻿—if I supposed that my son could make light of the orders with whose fulfilment he has been charged, I myself would tie him and drag him before the council of war that he might get his just reward.”

===Chinese===

“这都是怎么回事？”蒙特里亚愕然喊道，“奥古斯丁，你对这姑娘说过，你想玩忽职守吗？你认识她？”

奥古斯丁被恐惧压倒，一言不答。

“会的，他会放他自由的，”玛丽基亚绝望地说，“走开，先生们。这里没有你们的事。”

“我该怎么理解这件事？”唐·何塞喊道，一把抓住儿子的胳膊，“倘若这姑娘说的是真的，倘若我能想象我儿子的荣誉会这样扫地、他向军旗宣过的忠诚会被踩进泥里——倘若我想到我儿子会把他奉命执行的差事视同儿戏，我就亲手捆了他，拖到军事法庭去，叫他领受他应得的惩处。”
===Original===

“Señor, oh, my father,” said Augustine, pale as death, “I have never thought of failing in my duty.”

“Is that your father?” said Mariquilla. “Augustine, tell him that you love me, and perhaps he will have compassion on me.”

“This girl is mad,” said Don José. “Unhappy child, your trouble touches my heart. I charge myself with protecting you in your orphanhood. Yes, I will protect you as long as you reform your habits. Poor little one, you have a good heart, an excellent heart. But﻿—yes﻿—I have heard, a little inclined to be giddy. It is a pity that by being badly brought up a good soul should be lost. But you will be good? I think you will!”

“Augustine, how can you permit me to be insulted?” said Mariquilla, with overwhelming grief.

“It is not insult,” said the father, “it is good counsel. How could I insult my benefactress? I believe that if you behave yourself well, we shall have a great affection for you. Remain under my protection, poor orphan. Why do you talk so to my son? It is nothing, nothing; have better sense; and enough for now of all this agitation. The lad perhaps knows you. Yes, I have been told that during the siege you have not left the company of the soldiers. Now you must reform. I charge myself﻿—I cannot forget the kindness I have received. And besides I know that you are good at heart. That is not a deceitful face. You have a heavenly form. But it is necessary to renounce worldly enjoyments, refrain from vice﻿—then﻿—”

“No!” cried Augustine, suddenly, with so lively an outburst of anger that all of us trembled at seeing him and hearing him. “No! I will not consent that anyone, not even my father, should insult her before me. I love her! And if I have concealed it before, I tell it now, without fear or shame, for all the world to know! Sir, you do not know what you are saying, nor how you miss the truth! You have been deceived. You may kill me, if I fail in respect, but do not defame her before me; because if I should hear again what I have heard, not even the fact that you are my own father could restrain me!”

===Chinese===

“先生——噢，父亲，”奥古斯丁面如死灰地说，“我从来没有想过玩忽职守。”

“这就是你的父亲？”玛丽基亚说，“奥古斯丁，告诉他你爱我，也许他会可怜我。”

“这姑娘疯了，”唐·何塞说，“不幸的孩子，你的苦楚打动我的心。我认下这担子：在你孤苦无依时保护你。是的，我会保护你，只要你改掉你的习性。可怜的小东西，你有一颗好心，一颗极好的心。可是——是的——我听说过，就是有点轻浮。好好一个灵魂，只因教养失当便要糟蹋了，实在可惜。不过你会学好的，对不对？我想你会的！”

“奥古斯丁，你怎么能任凭人家这样作践我？”玛丽基亚说，悲不自胜。

“这不是作践，”做父亲的说，“这是金玉良言。我怎么会侮辱我的恩人？我相信，只要你行为端正，我们会十分疼爱你的。留在我的保护之下吧，可怜的孤儿。你为什么这样跟我儿子说话？没什么，没什么；放明白些；这阵折腾到此为止吧。这小伙子也许认识你。是的，我听人说，围城这些日子，你一直没离开过那些大兵的圈子。如今你必须改过。这事我认下了——我忘不了我受的恩惠。况且我知道你心地是好的。那不是一张会骗人的脸。你有天仙一般的容貌。但必须舍弃尘世的欢娱，戒绝罪愆——那么——”

“不！”奥古斯丁突然喊道，那一阵怒火如此炽烈，见到他、听到他声音的我们都浑身一颤。“不！我不能容许任何人——哪怕是我的父亲——当着我的面侮辱她。我爱她！从前我若有过隐瞒，如今我毫无畏惧、毫无羞愧地说出来，让全世界都知道！先生，您不知道自己在说些什么，也不知道您离真相有多远！您是受了欺瞒。我若有失敬之处，您尽可杀了我，只是别当着我的面污蔑她；因为倘若再让我听见方才那样的话，哪怕您是我的亲生父亲，也拦不住我了！”

===Original===

Montoria, not expecting this, looked about in amazement at his friends.

“Good, Augustine!” cried Mariquilla. “Do not pay any attention to these people. This man is not your father. Do what your heart tells you to do. Go away, señors! Go away!”

“You are mistaken, Mariquilla,” replied the young man; “I have not intended to free the prisoner, nor shall I do so; but at the same time I tell you that it will not be I who will take his life. There are officers in my battalion who will carry out the order. I am no longer a soldier. Although we are in the face of the enemy, I break my sword, and hasten to the Captain-General that he may decide my fate.”

As he said this, he drew his sword, and, doubling the blade across his knee, he broke it, and after throwing the two pieces into the middle of our circle, he went without another word.

“I am all alone! There is no one to help me!” cried Mariquilla, faintly.

===Chinese===

蒙特里亚没料到这一出，愕然环顾身旁的同僚。

“说得好，奥古斯丁！”玛丽基亚喊道，“别把这帮人放在心上。这个人不是你父亲。照你心里吩咐的去做吧。走开，先生们！走开！”

“你错了，玛丽基亚，”青年答道，“我并不曾打算放走囚犯，往后也不会放；可我同时告诉你，取他性命的也不会是我。我营里自会有军官去执行那道命令。我不再是军人了。虽说我军当面就是敌人，我仍要折断我的剑，即刻去见总司令，听凭他发落我的命运。”

说着，他抽出佩剑，将剑身横抵膝上折弯，喀的一声折为两段，把断剑扔进我们围成的圈子中央，便一言不发地走了。

“我孤零零一个人！没有人肯帮我！”玛丽基亚声气微弱地喊道。

===Original===

“Gentlemen, pay no attention to the affairs of my son. I will take that upon myself. Perhaps the girl has interested him. That is of little consequence. These inexperienced ecclesiastics are very likely to be taken in. And you, Señora Doña Mariquilla, try to calm yourself. We will look after you. I promise you that, if you behave yourself, you will later enter into repentance. Come, let us take her away from here!”

“No, no! nobody shall tear me away from here, except in bits,” said the girl, with the calmness of despair. “Oh, Señor Don José de Montoria, will you not ask them to pardon my father? If he would not forgive you, I forgive you a thousand times. But﻿—”

“I cannot do what you ask of me,” said the patriot, sadly. “The crime committed is enormous. You must go away. What terrible grief! It is necessary to resign yourself. God will pardon you all your faults, poor orphan. Rely upon me, and all that I can do﻿—we will take care of you. We will help you. I am moved not by gratitude alone but by pity. Come, come with me. It lacks only a quarter to ten.”

“Señor Montoria,” said Mariquilla, kneeling before the patriot, and kissing his hands, “you have influence in the city, and can save my father. You are angry with me because Augustine said he loved me. No, I will not love him. I will not see him any more. I am an honest girl; but he is above me, and I cannot think of marrying him. Señor de Montoria, by the soul of your dead son, help me! My father is innocent. No, it is not possible that he could have been a traitor. If the Holy Spirit should tell me, I would not believe it. They say that he was no patriot. I say it is a lie. They say that he did not give anything for the war; but now everything that we have shall be given. There is a great deal of money buried in the cellar of the house. I will tell you where it is, and they can take it all. They say that he has not taken up arms. I will take arms now. I am not afraid of the balls. The noise of the cannon does not terrify me. I am not afraid of anything. I will run to the places of greatest danger, and there, where the men can do nothing, I will go into the fire. I will dig in the mines with my own hands, and make holes for the powder under all the ground occupied by the French. Tell me if there is some castle to take, or some wall to defend; because I fear nothing, and of all living beings in Saragossa, I shall be the last to surrender.”

“Unhappy girl!” said the patriot, lifting her from the ground, “let us go, let us go from here!”

===Chinese===

“诸位先生，不必把我儿子的事情放在心上。那由我来承担。多半是这姑娘勾动了他的心。那也算不得什么。这些没见过世面的教门子弟，最容易上这种当。还有您，堂娜·玛丽基亚，请想法定一定神。我们会照看您的。我答应您，只要您行为检点，日后自会悔改归正。来，把她从这儿带走！”

“不，不！谁也别想把我从这儿弄走，除非把我撕成碎块，”姑娘说，带着绝望之人才有的平静。“噢，唐·何塞·德·蒙特里亚先生，您不愿求他们饶恕我父亲吗？他纵然不肯饶恕您，我可是已一千次地饶恕您了。可是——”

“你求我的事，我办不到，”这位爱国者悲伤地说，“犯下的罪太重了。你必须走了。何等可怕的悲痛！你要认命。上帝会赦免你一切的过犯，可怜的孤儿。信赖我——凡我力所能及，我们会照看你。我们会帮助你。打动我的不只是感激，还有怜悯。来，跟我来。差一刻就十点了。”

“蒙特里亚先生，”玛丽基亚跪在这位爱国者面前，亲吻着他的双手，“您在这城里有威望，您能救我父亲。您在生我的气，因为奥古斯丁说他爱我。不，我不再爱他了。我再也不见他。我是个清白的姑娘；可他门第高过我，我绝计想不到嫁给他。蒙特里亚先生，凭您亡故儿子的灵魂，救救我！我父亲是无辜的。不，他不可能当过叛徒。哪怕圣神亲口告诉我，我也不信。他们说他不爱国。我说那是谎话。他们说他没为战争出过什么力；可如今我们所有的一切，都可以交出来。家里的地窖里埋着许多许多钱。我告诉您埋在哪儿，他们可以统统拿去。他们说他没拿起过武器。我现在就拿起武器。我不怕枪弹。大炮的轰鸣吓不倒我。我什么都不怕。哪里最危险，我就往哪里跑；男人们无能为力的地方，我就冲进火里去。我要亲手去挖坑道，在法国人占据的每一寸地皮底下凿出装火药的洞来。告诉我，还有什么城堡要攻取，还有什么城墙要守卫；因为我什么都不怕，萨拉戈萨所有活着的人里头，我将是最后一个投降的。”

“不幸的姑娘！”爱国者说着，把她从地上搀起来，“走吧，我们离开这儿吧！”

===Original===

“Señor de Araceli,” said the head of our forces, who was present, “as Captain Augustine Montoria is not in his place, you are entrusted with the command of this company.”

“No, assassins of my father!” exclaimed Mariquilla, furious as a lion; “you shall not kill the innocent! Cowards! Executioners! You are the traitors, not he! You cannot conquer your enemies, so you enjoy taking life from an unfortunate old man. Soldiers, how can you talk of your honor, when you do not know what honor is? Augustine, where art thou! Señor Don José de Montoria, this is a contemptible vengeance planned by you, a spiteful and heartless man! My father has done wrong to no one, and you tried to rob him. He was right in not wishing to give you his flour, for you who call yourselves patriots are tradesmen who speculate in the misfortunes of the city. I cannot extort from these cruel men one compassionate word. Men of brass, barbarians! My father is innocent, and if he were not, he would have done well in selling such a city. They would easily give more than you are worth. But is there not one, one single one, to pity him and me?”

“Come, let us take her away, let us carry her off, señors,” said Montoria. “This cannot be prolonged. What has my son done with himself?”

They took her away, and for a time I could hear her heartrending cries.

“Good night, Señor de Araceli,” said Montoria to me. “I am going to see if I can get a little wine and water for this poor orphan.”

===Chinese===

“阿拉塞利先生，”在场的我军首长说，“奥古斯丁·蒙特里亚上尉既已不在其位，这连队的指挥就交托给您了。”

“不，杀害我父亲的凶手们！”玛丽基亚像母狮一般狂怒地喊道，“你们杀不成这个无辜的人！胆小鬼！刽子手！叛徒是你们，不是他！你们打不赢自己的敌人，就靠取一个倒霉老头儿的性命来取乐。士兵们，你们连荣誉是什么都不知道，还侈谈什么荣誉？奥古斯丁，你在哪儿啊！唐·何塞·德·蒙特里亚先生，这是你策划的一场卑鄙报复，你这个狠毒没心肝的人！我父亲没有害过任何人，倒是你们想抢夺他。他不肯把面粉给你，他做得对，因为你们这帮自称爱国者的家伙，全是发国难财的奸商。从这些狠心人嘴里，我一个怜悯的字也榨不出来。铜铸铁打的人，野蛮人！我父亲是无辜的；就算他不是，把这样一座城市卖掉，他也做得漂亮。人家轻轻松松就能出比你们身价更高的价钱。难道就没有一个人——哪怕只有一个——来可怜可怜他和我吗？”

“来，把她带走，把她架走，诸位先生，”蒙特里亚说，“不能再这样拖下去了。我儿子上哪儿去了？”

他们把她带走了，有一阵子，我还听得见她撕心裂肺的哭喊。

“晚安，阿拉塞利先生，”蒙特里亚对我说，“我去看看，能不能给这可怜的孤儿弄一点酒和水来。”

## XXX / 第三十章
===Original===
Horrible nightmare, leave me! I do not wish to sleep. But the bad dream which I long to fling from my remembrance returns to distress me. I wish I could blot from my memory the melancholy scene. But one night passes, and then another, and the scene is not blotted out. I, who on so many occasions have faced great dangers without winking an eyelash, I tremble now, and the cold sweat comes on my forehead. The sword bathed in French blood falls from my hand, and I shut my eyes in order not to see what passes before me. In vain I hurl thee away, dreadful vision! I expel thee, and thou dost return. Thou art fast rooted in my memory. No, I am not capable of taking the life of a fellow-being in cold blood, though inexorable duty commands it. Why did I not tremble in the trenches as I tremble now? I feel a mortal chill. By the light of lanterns I see sinister faces, one above all livid and sullen, that shows a terror greater than all other terrors. How the barrels of the guns gleam! All is ready, and but one word is lacking, my word. I try to pronounce the word, and I bite my tongue. No, that word will never come from my lips!

Away from me, black nightmare! I shut my eyes. I draw my eyelids closer, better to exclude thee, and the closer they are shut the plainer I see thee, horrible picture! They all wait with anxiety; but nothing is comparable to the state of my soul, rebelling against the law which obliges it to decide the end of another’s existence. Time passes, then eyes which I wish I had never seen disappear under the bandage. I cannot look at the scene; would that they had put a bandage over my eyes also! The soldiers look at me, and I frown to hide my cowardice. We mortals are stupid and vain even in supreme moments. The bystanders jested at my state, and that gave me a certain energy. I unglued my tongue from my palate, and cried﻿—

“Fire!”

The accursed nightmare will not go, and torments me tonight as it did last night, bringing again before me that which I do not wish to see. It is better not to sleep. I prefer wakefulness to this. I shake off the lethargy, and dread my vigil as before I abhorred the dream. Always the same humming of the cannon. Those insolent brass mouths do not cease to talk.
===Chinese===
骇人的梦魇，离开我吧！我不想睡。可那场我巴不得从记忆里抛却的噩梦，偏偏一次次回来折磨我。但愿我能把这凄惨的一幕从记忆中抹去。然而一夜过去，又一夜过去，那一幕总也抹不掉。我这个人，多少回直面大险大难连眼皮都不曾眨一下，此刻却浑身发颤，额角沁出冷汗。那柄浸过法国人鲜血的剑从我手中跌落，我闭上眼睛，不愿看见眼前掠过的景象。可怕的幻影，我把你掷出去也是枉然！我将你驱逐，你又归来。你已深深扎根在我的记忆里。不，我做不到冷血地夺走一个同类的性命，哪怕无情的职责命我如此。当初在堑壕里，我何曾像现在这样发过抖？我感到一阵致命的寒意。一盏盏灯笼的光里，我看见一张张阴森的脸，其中一张格外铁青阴沉，那上面写着的恐惧，比天下一切恐惧加起来还要深。枪管多么亮！一切俱已就绪，只欠一句话，我的一句话。我想把那句话说出口，却咬住了自己的舌头。不，那句话永远不会从我嘴里说出来！

滚开吧，黑色的梦魇！我闭上眼睛。我把眼睑阖得更紧，好把你挡在外面，可眼睛闭得越紧，越把你看得真切，可怖的画面！他们全都焦灼地等待着；但什么都比不上我灵魂此刻的煎熬——它在反抗那条律令，那条逼着它裁决另一个生命存亡的律令。时间一点点过去，随后，那双我但愿从未见过的眼睛，消失在蒙眼的布带之下。我不忍看那一幕；但愿他们连我的眼睛也蒙上！士兵们望着我，我皱起眉头，好遮掩自己的怯懦。我们这些凡人，即便到了最严峻的关头，也还是又愚蠢又虚荣。旁观的人拿我这副模样打趣，那反倒给了我几分气力。我把舌头从上颚上松开，喊出——

“开火！”

这场可咒的梦魇不肯散去，今夜还像昨夜一样折磨我，把我并不想看见的景象重新推到我眼前。还是不睡为好。与其这样，我宁愿醒着。我抖落昏沉，随即又害怕起这守夜来，一如方才我憎恶那梦境。炮声永远是同一种嗡鸣。那些傲慢无礼的黄铜大口，一刻也不肯住口。
===Original===
Ten days pass, and Saragossa has not yet surrendered, because some madmen are still persistent in guarding for Spain that heap of dust and ashes. The houses go on falling; and France, after establishing one foot, wastes armies and quintals of powder in gaining ground on which to set the other. Spain will not give up as long as she has one paving-stone to serve as a lever for the immense machine of her bravery. I am almost lifeless. I cannot move. Those men I see passing before me do not seem to be men. They are languid and emaciated, and their faces would be yellow, if dust and powder had not blackened them. Eyes gleam under blackened eyebrows﻿—eyes that do not yet know how to look without taking aim. Men are covered with unclean rags, and cloths are bound about their heads. They are so filthy that they seem like the dead raised from that heap in the Calle de la Imprenta, to show themselves among the living. From time to time among the smoky columns these dying ones come, and the friars murmur religious consolation to them. Neither the dying understand, nor the friar knows what he says. Religion itself goes half mad. Generals, soldiers, peasants, priests, and women are all overwhelmed. There are no classes or sexes. The city is defended in anarchy.

I do not know what happened me. Do not ask me to go on with the story, for there is nothing more to tell. That which I see before my memory does not seem real, the true things being confused in my memory with those dreamed.

I was stretched out in a gateway of the Calle de la Albardería, shaking with cold, my left hand wrapped in a bloody, dirty cloth. The fever burned me, and I longed for strength to hasten to the front. They were not all corpses beside me. I reached out my hand and touched the arm of a friend who was still living.
===Chinese===
十天过去了，萨拉戈萨仍未投降，因为还有些疯子，执意替西班牙守住那一堆尘土和灰烬。房屋接连倒塌；法兰西在站稳了一只脚之后，还在挥霍大批军队、一公担又一公担的火药，只为夺得一块能放下另一只脚的地皮。西班牙绝不会屈服，只要她还剩一块街石，可以充作她那部庞大勇气机器的杠杆。我几乎已经不省人事。我动弹不得。眼前走过的那些人，似乎已不成人形。他们孱弱干瘦，脸上本该是蜡黄的，若不是尘土和硝烟把它们熏黑了。熏黑的眉毛底下，眼睛闪着幽光——那是一双双除了瞄准还不会旁视的眼睛。人们裹着污秽的破布，头上缠着布巾。他们浑身腌臜，活像从因普伦塔街那座尸堆里爬起来的死人，到活人中间来显一显形。烟柱之间，不时走来这些濒死的人，修士们对着他们喃喃念诵宗教的慰藉。濒死的人听不懂，修士自己也不知道自己在说什么。宗教本身也已半疯了。将军、士兵、农夫、神父、妇女，一概被压垮了。没有阶级，也没有男女。这座城，是在一片无序的混沌中防守着的。

我不知道自己出了什么事。别再要我往下讲了，因为已经没有什么可讲的了。浮现在我记忆眼前的景象，仿佛并不真实；真事在我的记忆里，已经同梦里的幻象混作一团。

当时我瘫躺在阿尔巴德利亚街的一个门洞里，冷得直打哆嗦，左手裹着一条血迹斑斑的脏布。热病烧着我，我只盼着有力气赶赴前线。身旁躺着的并不全是尸体。我伸出手去，碰到一位还活着的友人的手臂。
===Original===
“What is going on, Señor Sursum Corda?”

“It seems that the French are on this side of the Coso,” he answered me, in a feeble voice. “They have blown up half of the city. May be we shall have to surrender. The Captain-General has fallen ill with the epidemic, and is in the Calle de Predicadores. They think he is going to die. The French will enter. I rejoice that I shall die before I see that. How do you find yourself, Señor de Araceli?”

“Very bad off. I will see if I can get up.”

“I am alive yet, it seems. I did not think I should be. The Lord be with me, I shall go straight to heaven. Señor de Araceli, have you died yet?”
===Chinese===
“出什么事了，Sursum Corda 先生？”

“看样子，法国人已经打到科索大街这一边了，”他有气无力地答我，“他们把半座城都炸塌了。说不定我们只好投降了。总司令染上了时疫，眼下在普雷迪卡多雷斯街（Calle de Predicadores），人们都当他要死了。法国人就要进城了。我庆幸自己会在看见那场面之前先死。您觉得自己怎么样，阿拉塞利先生？”

“糟得很。我看看还能不能站起来。”

“看来我还活着。我原本不指望自己还能活。愿主与我同在，我就要径直上天堂了。阿拉塞利先生，您死了没有？”
===Original===
I got up and took a few steps. Leaning against the walls, I advanced a little and came to the Orphanage. Some military officers of high rank were accompanying a short, slender ecclesiastic to the door, who dismissed them, saying, “We have done our duty, and human strength can compass nothing more.” It was Father Basilio. A friendly arm held me up, and I recognized Don Roque.

“Gabriel, my friend,” he said to me, in deep affliction, “the city surrenders this very day.”

“What city?”

“This.”
===Chinese===
我站起身，走了几步。倚着墙壁，我往前挪动了一程，来到孤儿院。几名高级军官正把一位矮小瘦削的教士送到门口；教士打发他们回去，说道：“我们已经尽了职责，人的力量所能做的，到此为止了。”那是巴西利奥神父。一条友好的手臂搀住了我，我认出是唐·罗克。

“加夫列尔，我的朋友，”他悲恸万状地对我说，“这座城就在今天投降了。”

“哪座城？”

“这座。”
===Original===
As he said so, it seemed to me as if nothing remained in its place. Men and houses all ran together confusedly. The Torre Nueva seemed to draw itself up to flee also, and in the distance its leaden casque fell from it. The flames of the city no longer gleamed. Columns of black smoke moved from east to west. Powder and ashes, raised by the whirling winds, moved in the same direction. The sky was no longer the sky, but a leaden canopy, strangely agitated.

“Everything is fleeing; everything is going from this place of desolation,” I said to Don Roque. “The French will find nothing.”

“Nothing. Today they enter by the Puerta del Angel. They say that the capitulation has been honorable. Look, here come the spectres who defend the plaza!”

Indeed along the Coso filed the last combatants, one for every thousand of those who had faced the bullets and the epidemic. There were fathers without sons, brothers without brothers, husbands without wives. He who cannot find his own among the living is not at all sure of finding them among the dead, because there are fifty-two thousand corpses, almost all piled in the streets, the doorways, the cellars, the ditches. The French, on entering, halted affrighted at such a spectacle, and were almost on the point of retreating. Tears streamed from their eyes, and they asked whether these were men or shadows, these poor creatures who fled at sight of them.
===Chinese===
他这么说着，我恍惚觉得天下再没有一样东西还待在原处了。人和房屋乱作一团，一齐奔逃。新塔似乎也挺直身子要跟着一起逃，远处，它头上那顶铅盔跌落了下来。城中的火焰已不再闪耀。一道道黑烟柱自东向西移动。旋风卷起的火药与灰烬，也朝同一方向飘去。天空已不成其为天空，成了一顶铅灰色的天盖，异样地鼓荡翻腾。

“一切都在逃散，一切都在离开这片荒凉之地，”我对唐·罗克说，“法国人将什么也找不到。”

“什么也找不到。他们今天就从安赫尔门（Puerta del Angel）进城。据说投降协定还算是体面的。瞧，守卫这座要塞的幽灵们来了！”

果然，沿着科索大街鱼贯而行的，是最后一批战斗者——在当年迎着枪弹、扛住时疫的人们当中，每一千人里才剩得下这一个。有失去了儿子的父亲，有失去了兄弟的兄弟，有失去了妻子的丈夫。在活人里寻不见亲人的人，也全无把握能在死人堆里寻见，因为城里横陈着五万二千具尸体，几乎全都堆在街上、门洞里、地窖里、沟渠里。法军进城时被这幅景象骇得止住了脚步，几乎就要退出去。他们热泪横流，问这些一见到他们就躲开的可怜生灵，究竟是人是影。
===Original===
A volunteer on entering his house stumbled over the bodies of his wife and children. A wife ran to the wall, to the trench, to the barricade to look for her husband; but no one knew where he was. The thousands of the dead did not speak; and could not tell whether her Fulano was among them. Many large families were exterminated, not one member was left. This saves many tears when death strikes with one blow the father and the orphan, the husband and the widow, the victim and the eyes that would have been forced to weep.

France had at last set foot within that city built on the banks of the classic river which gives its name to our peninsula.

They had conquered it without subduing it. On seeing the desolation of Saragossa, the Imperial army considered itself the gravediggers of the heroic inhabitants, instead of their conquerors. Fifty-three thousand lives were contributed by this Aragonese city to those of the millions of creatures wherewith humanity paid for the military glories of the French Empire. This sacrifice will not prove fruitless, for it was a sacrifice for an idea. The French Empire﻿—a vain thing, a thing of circumstance, founded on fickle fortune, in audacity and the military genius that is always a second-rate quality when separated from service of the ideal﻿—this empire existed merely by its own self-worship. The French Empire﻿—I say, that tempest which disturbed the first years of the century, and whose lightnings and thunderbolts held Europe in terror﻿—passed, as tempests pass. The normal state in historic life, as in nature, is that of calm. We all saw it pass, and we viewed its death-agony in 1815. We saw its resurrection a few years afterwards, but that also passed, overthrown by its own weight of pride. Perhaps this old tree will sprout the third time; but it will not give grateful shade to the world during centuries, and will scarcely serve for mankind to warm itself by its last bits of wood.

That which has not passed, nor shall pass, is the idea of nationality which Spain defended against the right of conquest and usurpation. When other peoples succumbed, she maintained her right, defended it, and, sacrificing her own lifeblood, hallowed it as martyrs hallowed the Christian idea in the arena. The result is that Spain, depreciated unjustly in the Congress of Vienna, disprized with reason for her civil wars, her bad governors, her disorders, her bankruptcy more or less declared, her immoral treaties, her extravagances, her bullfights, and her proclamations, has never since 1808 seen the continuation of her nationality placed in any doubt. Even today, when it seems that we have reached the last degree of abasement, offering more chance than Poland for dismemberment, no one dares attempt the conquest of this house of madmen. Men of little sense﻿—without any on occasion﻿—the Spanish will today, as ever, die a thousand deaths, stumbling and rising in the struggle of their inborn vices with the great qualities which they still preserve, with those which they acquire slowly, and those which Central Europe sends them. Providence holds in store for this people great advancings and abasements, great terrors and surprises, apparent deaths and mighty resurrections. Her destiny is to be able to live in agitation like a salamander in fire; but her national permanency is and ever will be assured.
===Chinese===
一名义勇军士兵走进自家家门，被妻儿的尸体绊了一跤。一个妻子奔到城墙边、堑壕里、街垒上去寻找丈夫；可谁也不知道他在哪里。成千上万的死者默然无语，说不出她家那位“某某君”（Fulano）是否就在其中。许多大家庭满门覆灭，不曾剩下一个人。这倒省下了许多眼泪——当死亡一击之下，同时结果了父亲与孤儿、丈夫与寡妇、受难者与那双本将被迫为他垂泪的眼睛。

法兰西终于把脚踏进了那座城——它建在一条古典时代名川的岸边，我们整个半岛的名字，正是得自这条河（埃布罗河，Ebro）。

他们征服了它，却没有降服它。目睹萨拉戈萨的满目疮痍，帝国军队自觉是这些英雄居民的掘墓人，而不是他们的征服者。这座阿拉贡的城，把五万三千条生命，添进了人类为法兰西帝国的军事荣光所偿付的那千百万生灵之中。这牺牲不会落空，因为这是为一种理念作出的牺牲。法兰西帝国——一桩虚浮之物，一桩乘时势而生之物，建立在反复无常的命运之上，建立在胆大妄为与军事天才之上，而军事天才一旦脱离了为理想服务，永远只是一种二流的品质——这个帝国，全凭对它自己的崇拜而存在。法兰西帝国——我是说，那场搅扰了本世纪最初年月的暴风雨，它的电闪雷霆曾令整个欧洲胆寒——过去了，正如暴风雨终会过去。历史生活中的常态，一如自然界的常态，是平静。我们都看见了它的消逝，在 1815 年亲眼看着它垂死挣扎。几年之后，我们又看见了它的复活，可那也过去了，被它自身骄傲的重量压垮。这棵老树也许还会第三度抽芽；但它再不能一连数百年为世界洒下宜人的浓荫，至多不过让人类拿它最后的几块碎木片，凑着烤一烤火罢了。

从未过去、也永不会过去的，是那民族性的理念——西班牙曾挺身捍卫它，以对抗征服与僭夺的权柄。当其他民族相继屈服时，她坚守自己的权利，捍卫它，并牺牲自己的生命膏血为它祝圣，一如殉道者在竞技场中祝圣了基督教的理念。其结果是：西班牙——她在维也纳会议上被不公正地贬抑，又因内战、因昏庸的统治者、因纷乱、因半公开的破产、因背弃道义的条约、因奢靡无度、因斗牛和浮夸文告而被有理有据地轻视——自 1808 年以来，却从未见过自己民族的存续受过半点怀疑。甚至就在今天，当我们似乎已跌到屈辱的最底一层、比波兰更有被瓜分的余地的时候，也没有人敢尝试征服这座疯人之家。西班牙人识见浅短——有时甚至全无识见——可他们今天仍一如往昔，甘愿死上一千回，在与生俱来的恶习同种种伟大品质的搏斗中跌倒又爬起；那些品质，有的是他们至今仍保存着的，有的是他们缓慢习得的，有的是中欧送到他们中间来的。天意为这个民族储下了大奋进与大沉沦、大恐怖与大惊奇、假死与大复起。她的命定，是能够像火中的蝾螈（salamander）一样在动荡中活下去；而她民族的绵延不绝，现在是、将来也永远是确凿无疑的。

## XXXI / 第三十一章

===Original===
It was the twenty-first day of February. A man whom I did not know came up to me, and said﻿—

“Come, Gabriel, I have need of thee.”

“Who are you?” I asked him. “I do not recognize you.”

“I am Augustine Montoria,” he answered. “Am I so much disfigured? They told me yesterday that you were dead. How I envied you! I see that you are as unfortunate as I, and that you are living still. Do you know, my friend, what I have just seen? The body of Mariquilla. It is in the Calle de Antón Trillo, at the entrance of the garden. Come, and we will bury her.”

===Chinese===
那是 2 月 21 日。一个我不认识的人走到我跟前，说道——

“来吧，加夫列尔，我用得着你。”

“你是谁？”我问他，“我认不出你了。”

“我是奥古斯丁·蒙特里亚，”他答道，“我变得这么厉害，都认不出了么？昨天他们告诉我，说你死了。我当时多么羡慕你啊！如今看来，你同我一样命苦，却还活着。我的朋友，你可知道我方才看见了什么？玛丽基亚的遗体。就在安东·特里约街，花园入口的地方。来吧，我们去把她安葬。”

===Original===
“I am more in a condition to be buried myself than to bury anybody. Who does that now? Of what did this woman die?”

“Of nothing, Gabriel, of nothing.”

“That is a singular death. I do not understand it.”

“Mariquilla’s body shows no wounds, nor any of the signs which the epidemic leaves in the face. She lies as if she had fallen asleep. Her face rests upon the ground, and she holds her hands to her ears as if she were shutting out sounds.”

“She does well. The noise of the shooting disturbed her. It seems to me as if I could hear it yet.”

“Come with me and help me. I have here a spade.”

===Chinese===
“我这副身子，与其说能去埋人，不如说更该等人来埋。眼下这种时候，谁还做这个？这女人是死于什么的？”

“不是死于任何东西，加夫列尔，不是死于任何东西。”

“这死法可真稀奇，我不明白。”

“玛丽基亚的遗体没有一点伤痕，脸上也没有时疫留下的那些痕迹。她躺在那里，仿佛只是睡熟了。脸伏在地上，双手捂着耳朵，仿佛要把一切声响关在外面。”

“她做得对。枪声搅扰了她。我好像至今还能听见它。”

“跟我来，帮帮我。我这儿有一把铁锹。”

===Original===
I arrived with difficulty at the place where my friend and two other comrades conducted me. My eyes did not let me see anything very well, and I only saw a shadowy figure stretched out there. Augustine and the other two raised the body, phantom or reality, which was there. I believe I made out her face, and on seeing it a great darkness fell upon my soul.

“She has not the slightest wound,” said Augustine, “not one drop of blood is upon her. Her eyelids are not swollen like those of the people who died of the epidemic. Mariquilla has not died of anything. Can you see her, Gabriel? It seems as if this figure that I hold in my arms has never been alive. It seems as if she is a beautiful, waxen image that I have loved in my dreams, showing herself to me with life, speech, and action. Do you see her? I see that all the inhabitants of this street are dead. If they were alive, I would call them to tell them that I loved her. Why did I hide it like a crime? Mariquilla, my wife, why didst thou die, without wounds, without sickness? What is the matter? What was it? Where are you now? Are you thinking? Do you remember me? Do you know, perhaps, that I am living? Mariquilla, Mariquilla, why do I still have that which they call life, and you not? Where shall I find you, to hear you, to talk with you, and to come to you so that you may see me? Everything is dark around me since you have closed your eyes. How long will this night of my soul endure, this solitude in which you have left me? The earth is insupportable to me. Despair possesses my soul. In vain I call unto God that He fill it with Himself. God does not answer me, and since you have gone, Mariquilla, the universe is empty.”

As he said this, we heard a sound as of many people coming near.

===Chinese===
我跌跌撞撞，好不容易才走到我那位朋友和另外两名战友领我去的地方。我的眼睛已不让我看清什么，只望见一个黑影直挺挺地横在那里。奥古斯丁和另外两人抬起那具遗体——也不知是幻影还是实体。我想我认出了她的脸；而一看见那张脸，一片深重的黑暗便落到了我的灵魂上。

“她没有丝毫伤痕，”奥古斯丁说，“身上不见一滴血。她的眼睑也不像死于时疫的人那样浮肿。玛丽基亚不是死于任何东西。你看得见她么，加夫列尔？我怀中抱着的这个身躯，仿佛从来不曾活过。仿佛她是一尊美丽的蜡像，我在梦里爱过她，如今她显现在我眼前，有生命，有言语，有行动。你看见她了么？我看这条街上的人都死绝了。他们若还活着，我要唤他们来，告诉他们：我爱过她。为什么我把这份爱藏得像一桩罪？玛丽基亚，我的妻，你怎么就死了，无伤，无病？这是怎么回事？到底是为了什么？你如今在哪里？你还在思想么？你还记得我么？你可知道——兴许知道——我还活着？玛丽基亚，玛丽基亚，为什么我偏偏还保有人们所谓的生命，而你却没有了？我到哪里去找你，去听你，去同你说话，去走到你面前，让你看见我？自从你闭上眼睛，我四周的一切都黑了。我灵魂的这场黑夜还要持续多久？你抛下我的这份孤寂还要持续多久？这大地我已承受不住。绝望攫住了我的灵魂。我徒然呼求上帝，求他以自己来充满它。上帝不回答我；而你走了之后，玛丽基亚，整个宇宙都空了。”

他正这样说着，我们听见许多人朝这边走近的声响。

===Original===
“It is the French. They have taken possession of the Coso,” said one.

“Friends, dig this grave quickly,” said Augustine, speaking to his two comrades, who were digging a great hole at the foot of the cypress. “If not, the French will come, and will take her from us.”

A man advanced along the Calle de Antón Trillo, and, stopping beside the ruined wall, looked in. I saw him, and trembled. He was greatly changed, cadaverous, with sunken eyes and uncertain step. His glance was without brilliancy; his body was bent; and he seemed to have aged twenty years since last I saw him. His clothing was of rags stained with blood and mire. In another place, and at another time, he would have been taken for an octogenarian, come to beg alms. He came nearer to us, and said in a voice so feeble that we could scarcely hear﻿—

===Chinese===
“是法军。他们占领科索大街了。”有人说。

“朋友们，快把墓穴挖好。”奥古斯丁对他的两名战友说——他们正在柏树脚下挖一个大坑，“不然法军一到，会把她从我们手里夺走的。”

一个人沿着安东·特里约街走过来，在残墙边停下，朝里面张望。我看见了他，浑身一颤。他变得厉害：形容如死尸，眼窝深陷，脚步虚浮。他的目光黯淡无光，身子伛偻着，自上次见面以来，仿佛老了二十岁。他衣衫褴褛，沾满血污和泥泞。若在别的地方、别的年月，人家准会把他当成一个来讨施舍的八旬老翁。他朝我们走近，用微弱得几乎听不见的声音说道——

===Original===
“Augustine, my son, what are you doing here?”

“Señor, my father, I am burying Mariquilla,” replied Augustine, without emotion.

“Why are you doing that? Why such solicitude for a stranger? The body of your poor brother lies even now unburied among the patriots. Why have you separated yourself from your mother and your sister?”

“My sister is surrounded by kind and affectionate people to take care of her, while this one has nobody but myself.”

Don José de Montoria, more gloomy and thoughtful than I had ever seen him, said nothing, and began to throw earth into the grave where they had placed the body of the beautiful girl.

===Chinese===
“奥古斯丁，我的儿，你在这里做什么？”

“先生，我的父亲，我在安葬玛丽基亚。”奥古斯丁答道，不动声色。

“你为什么要做这个？对一个外人，何必这样上心？你那可怜哥哥的遗体，此刻还同爱国志士们躺在一处，无人掩埋呢。你怎么撇下了你的母亲和你的妹妹？”

“妹妹身边围着善良慈爱的人照料，而她，除了我再没有别人了。”

唐·何塞·德·蒙特里亚比我历来所见更加阴郁、更加心事重重，一言不发，动手往墓穴里填土——他们已把那美丽姑娘的遗体安放了进去。

===Original===
“Throw in earth, my son, throw in earth quickly!” he cried, at last. “All is indeed over. They have permitted the French to enter the city, when it might still have been defended a couple of months more. These people have no soul. Come with me, and we will talk about yourself.”

“Señor,” replied Augustine, in firm tones, “the French are in the city. The gates are left free. It is now ten, and at twelve I leave Saragossa to go to the Monastery de Veruela, where I shall stay until I die.”

The garrison, according to the stipulation, were to leave with military honors by the Puerta del Portillo. I was so ill, so weakened by a wound lately received, and by hunger and fatigue, my comrades almost had to carry me. I scarcely saw the French as with sadness rather than rejoicing they took possession of that which had been a city. It was a city of terrible ruins, a city of desolation, worthy to be mourned by Jeremiah or sung by Homer.

===Chinese===
“填土吧，我的儿，快填土！”他终于喊出声来，“一切当真完了。人家放法军进了城，其实这城本还可以再守上两个月。这些人没有灵魂。跟我来吧，我们谈谈你自己的事。”

“先生，”奥古斯丁语调坚定地答道，“法军已进了城。城门任人出入。现在十点，十二点我就离开萨拉戈萨，去贝鲁埃拉修道院，在那里待到死去。”

按照投降协定的规定，守军将循军礼自波蒂略门出城。我病得那样厉害，新近受的一处创伤，加上饥饿与劳顿，把我拖得虚弱不堪，战友们几乎是架着我走的。法军接管那座曾经是城市的地方时，神情与其说是欢欣，不如说是悲凉——这情景我几乎没能看清。那是一座骇人的废墟之城，一座凄凉之城，值得耶利米来为之哀哭，或荷马来为之歌吟。

===Original===
In the Muela, where I stopped to recover myself, Don Roque appeared. He was leaving the city, and feared being followed as a suspect.

“Gabriel,” he said to me, “I never believed that the French mob would be so vile. I hoped that in view of the heroic defence of the city, they would be more human. Some days ago we saw two bodies which the Ebro was hurrying along on its current. They were two victims of those murderous soldiers that Lannes commands. They were Santiago Sas, commander of those brave musketeers of the parish of San Pablo, and Father Basilio Boggiero, teacher, friend, and counsellor of Palafox. They say that they went and called up Father Basilio at midnight, pretending that they wished to entrust an important commission to him; and then they took him on their treacherous bayonets to the bridge, where they pierced him through, and flung him into the river. And they did the same with Sas.”

“And our protector and friend, Don José de Montoria, what of him?”

“Thanks to the efforts of the chief-justice, he is still alive; but they want to shoot me, if you please. Did you ever see such savages? Palafox, it seems, is being taken a prisoner to France, although they promised to respect his person. In short, my boy, this is a nation I should not like to meet in heaven. And what do you say to that little barrack-sergeant of a marshal, Señor Lannes? He does not lack impudence to do what he has done. He has taken the treasures of the Virgin del Pilar, saying that they were not safe in the church. After he saw such a quantity of precious stones, diamonds, emeralds, and rubies, it seems that they got into his eyes, so that he held on to them. In order to hide his plundering, he pretends that the junta has given them to him. Of a truth, I am sorry not to be young like yourself, so as to fight against such a highway robber. And so Montoria said also, when I took my leave of him. Poor Don José, how sad it is! I give him but few years of life. The death of his elder son, and the resolution of Augustine to become a priest, make him very downcast and extremely melancholy.”

===Chinese===
我在穆埃拉大道上停下来将养，唐·罗克出现了。他正离城而去，生怕被人当作嫌疑犯盯梢。

“加夫列尔，”他对我说，“我从来不信，法国匪兵竟能卑劣到这般地步。我原指望，念在这城守卫得如此英勇，他们多少会像个人样。前几天，我们看见两具尸体，埃布罗河的急流正卷着它们奔泻而下。那是拉纳麾下那些杀人凶兵造下的两名冤魂：一个是圣地亚哥·萨斯，圣巴勃罗教区那队英勇燧发枪手的指挥官；一个是巴西利奥·博希耶罗神父，帕拉福克斯的老师、朋友与顾问。据说，那些人半夜里去叫醒博希耶罗神父，假称有一桩要紧差事要托付他；随后把他架上他们那背信弃义的刺刀，押到大桥上，把他捅了个通透，扔进了河里。对萨斯，他们也是如法炮制。”

“那我们的恩人、我们的朋友唐·何塞·德·蒙特里亚，他怎么样了？”

“多亏高等法院院长奔走出力，他还活着；可是你瞧，人家倒要枪毙我呢。你见过这样的野蛮人么？看样子，帕拉福克斯要被当作俘虏押往法国了，尽管他们答应过保全他的人身。总而言之，我的孩子，这个民族，就是在天堂上我也不愿同他们相遇。还有那位拉纳先生，那个小小的兵营军士模样的元帅，你说他怎么样？他干得出这种事来，脸皮不可谓不厚。他把皮拉尔圣母的珍宝都拿走了，说什么存放在教堂里不安全。及至他见了那么一大宗宝石——钻石、祖母绿、红宝石——那些东西仿佛钻进了他的眼里，于是他就攥住不放了。为遮掩他这桩抢掠，他还假称是政务会送给他的。说实在的，我遗憾不能像你一样年轻，好去同这样一个拦路大盗拼一拼。我向蒙特里亚辞行的时候，他也是这么说的。可怜的唐·何塞，多么惨啊！我看他活不了几年了。长子之死，加上奥古斯丁决意出家做神父，使得他心灰意冷、忧郁到了极处。”

===Original===
Don Roque had stopped to keep me company for a little time. And now we separated.

After I recovered, I continued in the campaign of 1809, taking part in other battles, becoming acquainted with new people, and establishing new friendships, or renewing the old.

Later, I shall relate some things about that year, as Andresillo Marijuan told them to me, when I chanced upon him in Castile, as I was returning from Talavera and he from Gerona.

===Chinese===
唐·罗克停下来，陪了我一小会儿。如今我们分手了。

待我康复之后，我又投身 1809 年的战局，参加了另几场战役，结识了新的人物，结下了新的友谊，也重续了旧日的交情。

日后，我还要讲述那一年的若干事情，那是安德烈西约·马里胡安（Andresillo Marijuan）讲给我听的：我在卡斯蒂利亚偶然遇上他，那时我刚从塔拉韦拉回来，而他刚从赫罗纳回来。

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===
1. Today the Convent of Jerusalem still exists in a restored condition. Its façade is towards the Hall of Independence. The hospital occupied the place where the Hotel de l’Europe stands. The present Palace of Deputies for the Province was constructed on the site of the Convent of San Francisco. ↩︎

===Chinese===
1. 耶路撒冷修道院（Convent of Jerusalem）至今犹存，业经修缮。其正立面朝向独立厅（Hall of Independence）。当年的医院，就设在今日欧罗巴饭店（Hotel de l’Europe）矗立之处。如今的省议会大厦（Palace of Deputies for the Province），则建于圣方济各修道院（Convent of San Francisco）的旧址之上。 ↩︎
